# Work Package 2 — Conformance Requirements

**Status: WORKING DRAFT — CHUNK 7 CONFORMANCE REQUIREMENTS AND IMPLEMENTATION HANDOFF.** All eight sections of this document hold their substantive content as of Chunk 7 of the staged drafting, and that content awaits Advisor review. This document is the test document of the Work Package 2 specification package: it maps every normative requirement of the [main specification](./WORK_PACKAGE_002_MINIMUM_PARALLEL_RUN_LABORATORY_SPECIFICATION.md) — every **MUST** and **MUST NOT** requirement of its twelve namespaces — to at least one conceptual check, and it states the check contracts, fault-injection classes, suite structure, evidence contract, boundaries, and acceptance criteria a later authorized conformance assessment would apply.

Every identifier this document assigns — `CHK-###` for conceptual checks, `FLT-###` for fault-injection entries, `FIX-##` for fixtures, `CASE-##` for suite cases, and `CQ-##` for unresolved conformance questions — is a test-document identifier, not a normative requirement identifier. These identifiers belong to no `WP2-*` requirement namespace, carry no normative force of their own, and are visibly distinct from every requirement ID of the main specification. This document states no requirement of its own: its binding force flows entirely from the requirements of the main specification, Section 17 of which states the conformance model every assessment under this document has to satisfy, and it cites specification requirements rather than restating, weakening, or amending them.

Work Package 2 remains specification-only. No implementation, experiment, model call, evidence generation, conformance run, or Work Package 3 activity is authorized. Work Packages 3–8 remain unauthorized. Implementation requires a later, separate Operator authorization and applicable Advisor review. Completion of this document does not make the Work Package 2 documentation package accepted: the final integration audit, the assignment of the first established specification version, and full continuous integration on the exact final head remain assigned to Chunk 8, as Section 20 of the main specification states, and every requirement of the main specification remains provisional until that first established version is assigned, as its Section 1.4 states.

## 1. Conformance principles

This section states the principles that govern every other section of this document and every later authorized conformance assessment under it. The principles state no requirement: each is the operating consequence of the conformance model Section 17 of the main specification states, and where a principle names a specification requirement it cites the requirement rather than replacing it. Seven principles govern.

**Exact-version binding.** Conformance is a relation among three exact identities: one exact main-specification identity and version, one exact conformance-document identity and version, and one exact implementation build and version. Every check result, every requirement basis, and every whole-implementation verdict names all three, holds only for the exact triple it names, and is never inherited by a different specification version, conformance-document version, or implementation build. Section 1.4 of the main specification records that no established specification version exists at this head and assigns the first to the final integration chunk; until one exists, no conformance assessment can bind to one, and none is authorized, performed, or claimed.

**Evidence discipline.** Every recorded outcome rests on identified, preserved evidence, and an outcome without evidence is no outcome: a check result identifies the exact requirement and check it evaluates, the fixtures and injected fault where any, the implementation build, the specification and conformance-document versions, the environment, the outcome and its reason, and its source evidence, and Section 5 states the complete evidence contract. Every normative requirement is independently accountable: a requirement's basis is evaluable on its own even where several requirements share one check, one requirement maps to several checks where its clauses can fail independently, one check covers several requirements only where every requirement-to-check relation is explicit, and the Section 2 matrix states every such relation row by row. The seven check classes of the Section 17 model — static document audit, deterministic keyless execution, concurrency and race testing, restart and recovery testing, fake-provider testing, evidence-integrity testing, and later registered-study evidence — remain distinct, and every check names its class.

**Keyless boundary.** Implementation acceptance is assessable in full without a live provider credential, a repository game-model call, a browser experiment, or any Work Package 3 study. Every implementation-acceptance requirement has, in the Section 2 matrix, a keyless static or dynamic check capable of supporting its acceptance without a live provider. Later registered-study evidence may supplement such a check; it never substitutes for one, a supplement is identified as such wherever it appears, and no Work Package 3 parity check is a Work Package 2 implementation-acceptance pass condition.

**False-pass prevention.** Absence of a failure is not a pass, absent evidence is not a pass, an unrun check is not a pass, and a blocked check is not a pass. A check never passes because its trigger did not occur: a detection check whose fault was never injected, a race check whose material interleaving never arose, and a refusal check whose prohibited operation was never attempted have not run. A check covering race safety forces or systematically enumerates its material interleavings conceptually rather than relying on ordinary timing luck. Every check and every matrix row states a principal false-pass risk, and a clean baseline pass and successful detection of every required negative or fault case are separately necessary, neither substituting for the other, as Section 4 states for every suite case.

**Applicability rules.** Check outcomes distinguish at minimum pass, fail, blocked or not run, and explicitly justified inapplicability, and these outcomes are never collapsed into one scalar result. Inapplicability is available only where the governing requirement itself permits it — in the way WP2-EVID-025 keeps the absence of a UI replay surface from being a nonconformity — and only with an explicit recorded basis identifying the permitting requirement, the condition that satisfies it, and the observation from which that condition was confirmed for the assessed conduct, as WP2-HAND-008 requires. An inapplicability lacking that recorded basis, or one whose satisfying condition is asserted rather than confirmed from an identified observation, is a blocked check, not a pass.

**Preservation of failed checks.** A failed, blocked, or inapplicable outcome is preserved with its complete evidence exactly as recorded, and is never deleted, overwritten, normalized, or replaced under its own result identity. A rerun is a further identified result naming what it reruns and why, the earlier result remaining preserved, and the conformance history of an implementation build shows every recorded outcome rather than a favorable subset. Losing operations, injected-fault residue, and cleanup outcomes are preserved under the same discipline, as the Section 5 evidence contract states.

**Acceptance-versus-research separation.** A conformance verdict establishes infrastructure behavior only. It establishes no behavioral quality, believability, semantic correctness, or observer value, no scientific validity of any study, no serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, neutrality, or interchangeability, and no success of any candidate cognitive architecture, as Section 2.3 of the main specification states and as WP2-SCOPE-011 and WP2-SCOPE-012 bind for every report of a conformance result. Conformance evidence is not study evidence for a claim about a candidate cognitive architecture, and it is not Work Package 3 parity evidence, merely because it is sealed, complete, or reproducible; Section 6 states that boundary in full.

## 2. Requirement-to-check matrix

This section carries the complete requirement-to-check mapping of the conformance contract: Section 2.1 states how the matrix is read, Section 2.2 defines the conceptual check catalogue the matrix cites, and Section 2.3 states the matrix itself, one subsection per requirement namespace. Everything in this section is test-document content: the properties recorded here are properties any conformance assessment of a candidate implementation has to exhibit, and the obligations that make them binding are the main specification's, stated in specification Sections 17 and 18, which this document operationalizes and never restates, weakens, or amends.

### 2.1 How the matrix is read

The identifiers this document defines — `CHK-###` for conceptual checks, `FLT-###` for fault-catalogue entries, `FIX-##` for fixtures, `CASE-##` for suite cases, and `CQ-##` for unresolved conformance questions — are test-document identifiers, not normative requirement identifiers. They are visibly distinct from `WP2-HAND-###` and from every other `WP2-*` requirement namespace, they create no obligation of their own, and no sentence of this document is read as adding to, narrowing, or reinterpreting any requirement of the main specification. A check, fault, fixture, or suite case describes how a requirement is tested; the requirement text alone governs what conformance is.

**The row contract.** Section 2.3 contains exactly one explicit matrix row per requirement ID of the completed main specification, arranged in twelve namespace subsections in the order in which the specification introduces the namespaces, with `WP2-HAND` last. A row stands alone: it never relies on another row, on a range, on "same as above", on positional convention, or on any other implicit coverage. Every row supplies every one of the eleven columns below with row-specific content — in particular a row-specific conceptual pass condition and a row-specific principal false-pass risk, never a generic placeholder.

| Column | What the row states |
| --- | --- |
| Requirement ID | Exactly one requirement ID of the completed main specification. The set of IDs appearing in this column is exactly the specification's requirement-ID set, under the set-equality property below. |
| Behavior or invariant tested | A test-facing summary of the behavior or invariant the requirement makes falsifiable. The summary directs the check; it carries no normative force, and the requirement text alone governs. |
| Conceptual check ID(s) | One or more `CHK` identifiers defined in Section 2.2, each relationship stated in the row itself. A compound requirement's check list covers every independently falsifiable clause of the requirement; where clauses diverge in kind, the row cites several checks. |
| Keyless conformance-test class | The Section 2.2 catalogue class of the row's primary check — the first check the row cites; the classes of any further cited checks are read from the Section 2.2 catalogue. The later-registered-study-evidence class of Section 2.2.7 never appears in this column; a row's supplementary later-study check is disclosed in its Execution venue cell instead, and no row's acceptance basis rests on one. |
| Required fixture(s) | The `FIX` identities of the Section 4.2 fixture register that the row's checks assume for this requirement, or the explicit statement "None". |
| Required fault injection or explicit none | The `FLT` identities of the Section 3 catalogue that the row's checks inject for this requirement, or the explicit statement "None" for a clean-path row. The cell names the faults on which this requirement's own pass condition depends, which need not be every fault the row's cited checks inject for other requirements; it reads "None" where the requirement is established on a clean path, and a row whose primary check is a static document audit may carry the faults its further dynamic checks inject for it. |
| Expected observable evidence | The records, refusals, dispositions, preserved losing operations, or audit citations whose existence and content the row's checks read. |
| Conceptual pass condition | The conceptual condition under which the row's checks pass for this requirement, stated so that the absence of a failure, absent evidence, an unrun check, or a blocked check can never satisfy it. |
| Principal false-pass risk | The most plausible way the row could appear to pass while the requirement is in fact violated. |
| Execution venue | Static, local, CI, or later registered-study evidence — the venue of the row's primary check, a further cited check's differing venue named where the row discloses it; a later-registered-study-evidence venue is supplementary only. |
| Boundary | Exactly one of the two renderings boundary discipline below defines: "Implementation acceptance" or "Implementation acceptance (parity claim boundary)". |

Throughout the matrix, a Required fixture(s) cell cites the `FIX` identities of the Section 4.2 fixture register, with or without the register's short titles, and reads "None" where a check needs no fixture.

**Check reuse.** One conceptual check may serve several rows, and one row may cite several checks. Every such relationship is explicit: the row names each check it relies on, the check's own mapped-requirement field in Section 2.2 names the requirements the check principally tests and never lists as tested one no row maps to it, a field that expressly delegates a clause to another check naming that other check's relation rather than its own, and the complete authoritative relation for any check is the set of Section 2.3 rows citing it, which the field never contradicts. Reuse is never implicit — no row inherits a check from an adjacent row, a shared table, or a namespace default, and no check covers a requirement that no row maps to it.

**Set equality.** The requirement-ID set of Section 2.3 is exactly the requirement-ID set of the completed main specification. The eleven namespaces of specification Sections 3 through 16 contribute four hundred twenty-three requirements — sixteen `WP2-SCOPE`, twenty-seven `WP2-ID`, thirty-three `WP2-ISO`, thirty-five `WP2-LIFE`, fifty-three `WP2-SCHED`, twenty-three `WP2-TIME`, fifty-five `WP2-MODEL`, seventy-three `WP2-EVID`, forty-five `WP2-FAIL`, forty-four `WP2-AGG`, and nineteen `WP2-SEC` — and the `WP2-HAND` namespace of specification Sections 17 through 21 contributes forty-three, for four hundred sixty-six requirement identifiers in total. No ID is missing, no ID is duplicated, no ID appears that the specification does not define, no range stands in for enumerated requirements, and no independently falsifiable clause of a compound requirement is left without a check named in that requirement's row.

**Boundary discipline.** Every row's Boundary column reads exactly one of two renderings, "Implementation acceptance" or "Implementation acceptance (parity claim boundary)": the annotated rendering marks a requirement whose subject is the parity claim boundary itself — the non-inference of serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, neutrality, or interchangeability, and the separateness of the Work Package 3 study — whose documentation half is checked as implementation acceptance, and whose later-registered-study check, where its row cites one, is a supplement, so that no row's acceptance ever rests on a Work Package 3 parity result. Even an annotated row cites a static implementation-acceptance check of its documentation half, so that no row is testable only through a study that Work Package 2 cannot run. Wherever classification is arguable, the row's Boundary is the unannotated rendering.

**Keyless discipline.** Every row whose boundary is implementation acceptance cites at least one keyless static or dynamic check capable of supporting that requirement's acceptance without a live provider credential, repository game-model call, browser experiment, or Work Package 3 study. A later-registered-study-evidence check may supplement such a row and never silently substitutes for its keyless check, and no Work Package 3 parity check is a Work Package 2 implementation-acceptance pass condition.

### 2.2 The conceptual check catalogue

This catalogue defines every conceptual check the matrix cites: one hundred thirty-seven checks, `CHK-001` through `CHK-137`, in one flat, stable sequence that is never renumbered or reused, grouped into the seven check classes that specification Section 17 keeps distinct — twenty-one static document audit checks, forty-six deterministic keyless execution checks, nineteen concurrency and race checks, seven restart and recovery checks, eighteen fake-provider checks, twenty-three evidence-integrity checks, and three later-registered-study-evidence checks. Every check is conceptual: it names properties, operations, and observations, and it selects no test framework, concurrency primitive, queue, database, store, runner, archive format, cloud, provider, product, vendor, or deployment topology.

Each entry consists of a bold lead-in naming the check and a table of the same eleven fields in the same order: purpose; mapped requirement IDs; fixtures and setup; controlled operation or observation; injected fault or clean path; expected evidence; pass condition; fail and blocked conditions; principal false-pass risk; cleanup or preservation; and venue. Five conventions keep the entries compact and are read into every entry.

First, the mapped-requirement field names the principal requirement IDs the check tests. The complete requirement-to-check relation is the matrix's: every Section 2.3 row citing a check states its own relation, every ID a check lists appears in a row that cites that check, and the field never stands in for the rows.

Second, fixture references name fixture families; the Section 4 fixture register assigns each family its `FIX` identity, and the Section 4 suite cases bind them. The families are: the *synthetic registered-study fixture*, one complete synthetic registered study — registration, planned runs, conditions, treatments, and every registered policy the specification names, scheduling, liveness, cancellation, authoritative-commit, retry and fallback, presentation-disposition, validation, budget, replacement, materiality, evidence-contract, retention, secret-handling, and aggregation policies among them — entirely synthetic and provider-free; the *two-study fixture*, two such studies arranged to share every shareable infrastructure element; the *deterministic engine fixture*, a deterministic simulated-world engine consuming registered stochastic inputs and able to carry an execution attempt end to end without any provider; the *scripted fake-provider fixture*, a deterministic stand-in on the provider path whose responses, failures, delays, rate-limit responses, substitutions, and late completions are scripted by the check; the *synthetic credential fixture*, synthetic never-live credential material and nonsecret credential references, described rather than exemplified, carrying a detector-recognizable synthetic marker where a check exercises secret detection; the *controlled-interleaving harness*, a conceptual driver that forces or enumerates the material interleavings of named operations at a named authority boundary; the *crash-and-restart harness*, a conceptual driver that terminates and restarts a process, a laboratory-worker execution agent, or the orchestrator at declared points; the *prepared evidence corpus*, closed run-evidence roots, inventories, seals, packages, and verification records prepared to a known composition, deliberately defective variants among them; and the *record-audit corpus*, the candidate implementation's documentation, record and policy definitions, and reporting surfaces, assembled for static audit together with the exact main-specification and conformance-document versions bound.

Third, fault references name entries of the Section 3 fault-injection catalogue by `FLT` identity; every fault of that catalogue is referenced by at least one check of this catalogue, and a check that depends on no catalogued fault states its clean path, or the controlled adversarial operation it drives, explicitly. A forced interleaving of otherwise well-formed operations is a controlled interleaving, not a fault, and is stated as such.

Fourth, one blocked rule holds for every check: where a fixture or prerequisite cannot be established, the controlled operation cannot be driven, or the subject artifact does not exist, the outcome is blocked or not run — recorded as such and never as a pass. No check passes merely because its trigger did not occur, and every check covering race safety forces or systematically enumerates its material interleavings conceptually rather than relying on ordinary timing luck. Entries state only check-specific fail conditions and any blocked circumstance beyond this general rule.

Fifth, one preservation rule holds for every check: every record, refusal, rejection, losing operation, and defective artifact a check produces or observes is preserved into the conformance-evidence package of Section 5, and no cleanup destroys the records under test. Entries state only treatment beyond this general rule.

#### 2.2.1 Static document audit checks (CHK-001 through CHK-021)

The checks of this class audit artifacts without executing anything: the two governing documents, the candidate implementation's documentation, record and policy definitions, and reporting surfaces. They carry the documentation half of the register-incorporation requirements and of the claim-boundary requirements, and they are the checks through which every `WP2-HAND` requirement of specification Sections 17 through 21 is testable without a study.

**CHK-001 — Exact-version binding and conformance-evidence contract audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the conformance assessment binds one exact main-specification identity and version, one exact conformance-document identity and version, and one exact implementation build and source identity, and that its planned evidence carries every identity the Section 5 evidence contract requires. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-001, WP2-HAND-004, WP2-HAND-015, WP2-HAND-016, WP2-HAND-025, WP2-HAND-030, WP2-HAND-035. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the assessment plan and every produced conformance record for the three bound identities and the Section 5 evidence fields, citing each artifact read. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record citing, for every conformance artifact, the exact specification, conformance-document, and build identities it carries. |
| Pass condition | Every conformance artifact carries all three exact identities, and no artifact binds a differing or unversioned identity. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any artifact omits an identity, carries a version-less reference, or binds a version other than the assessed one. Blocked: the assessment artifacts are not yet assembled. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Auditing document titles or claimed version strings instead of the content identity actually bound to each artifact. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the audit record itself enters the conformance-evidence package. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-002 — Matrix set-equality and coverage audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify mechanically that the Section 2.3 requirement-ID set equals the completed main specification's requirement-ID set in both directions, that every row cites at least one defined check, and that every independently falsifiable clause of every compound requirement has a check named in its row. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-002, WP2-HAND-003, WP2-HAND-004, WP2-HAND-005, WP2-HAND-006, WP2-HAND-019, WP2-HAND-033. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus, holding the exact bound versions of both governing documents. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Extract both requirement-ID sets; compute set differences; resolve every check reference of every row against Section 2.2; audit compound rows against their clause lists. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | The two extracted ID sets, both set differences, and a per-row check-resolution report. |
| Pass condition | Both set differences are empty, every ID appears exactly once, every cited check is defined, and no compound clause is uncovered. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any missing, duplicate, extra, or range-collapsed ID, any dangling check reference, or any uncovered clause. Blocked: either bound document version is unavailable. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Comparing row counts instead of ID sets, which a simultaneous omission and duplication leaves equal. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-003 — Outcome-vocabulary and false-pass discipline audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's conformance reporting distinguishes pass, fail, blocked or not run, and explicitly justified inapplicability without collapsing them into one scalar result, that absence of failure or evidence never appears as a pass, and that every recorded inapplicability names the governing requirement's own permitting clause and its recorded basis. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-004, WP2-HAND-005, WP2-HAND-007, WP2-HAND-008, WP2-HAND-009, WP2-HAND-011, WP2-HAND-013, WP2-HAND-015, WP2-HAND-016, WP2-HAND-023, WP2-HAND-025, WP2-HAND-036. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the outcome vocabulary of every conformance-result definition and every produced result record, and trace every inapplicability to its permitting requirement clause and recorded basis. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record enumerating the outcome values in use and every inapplicability with its basis. |
| Pass condition | All four outcome kinds are distinctly representable and used, no absent evidence or unrun check is recorded as a pass, and every inapplicability rests on a permitting clause with a recorded basis whose satisfying condition is confirmed from an identified observation rather than asserted. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a collapsed outcome scalar, a pass recorded from absence, or an inapplicability without a permitting clause or basis. Blocked: no result records exist yet to audit beyond the definitions. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Auditing the vocabulary definition while produced records silently map blocked outcomes onto pass. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-004 — Keyless acceptance-boundary audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that no implementation-acceptance pass condition anywhere in the assessment requires a live provider credential, a repository game-model call, a browser experiment, or a Work Package 3 study, and that every later-registered-study-evidence check is recorded as supplementary rather than substituting. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-010, WP2-HAND-011, WP2-HAND-012, WP2-HAND-013, WP2-HAND-014. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit every acceptance pass condition, every suite case, and every matrix row for prerequisites that are not keyless, and audit the recorded role of every later-registered-study-evidence check. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record listing every acceptance pass condition and its venue and prerequisites. |
| Pass condition | Every implementation-acceptance pass condition is satisfiable keylessly, and no supplementary evidence stands in for a keyless check. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any acceptance condition requiring live credentials, live model calls, browser experiments, or a study, or any silent substitution. Blocked: the assessment plan is not yet assembled. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A pass condition that is keyless as written but whose expected evidence is producible only by a live-provider run. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-005 — Terminology and vocabulary audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's reporting and record vocabulary uses the specification's defined terms with their assigned meanings — the Section 3.1 terms, the Section 8.3 lifecycle vocabulary and the state and disposition names of the two lifecycle models, and the five Section 16.2 secret-handling terms — with any predating machine-readable field name carried only alongside a retained explicit mapping. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCOPE-005, WP2-LIFE-002, WP2-SEC-001. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit record schemas, report templates, and produced reporting surfaces against the defined-term inventories, and audit every legacy field name for its retained mapping. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A term-by-term audit record citing each surface audited. |
| Pass condition | Every defined term is used with its assigned meaning, no two terms are treated as synonyms, and every legacy name carries its retained mapping. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a defined term used with a shifted meaning, two vocabulary values merged, or a legacy name without a mapping. Blocked: reporting surfaces do not yet exist. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Auditing schema definitions while free-text report prose uses lifecycle or secret-handling terms interchangeably. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-006 — Selection-prohibition audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that no reporting or documentation surface presents a current repository mechanism as a requirement, guarantee, or approved design of the future laboratory, cites the specification as selecting a technology or stack, or cites it as selecting, endorsing, or bounding any numeric value, threshold, limit, target, or scaling rule. |

| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |

| Pass condition | No surface presents a mechanism as specification-required, and no surface attributes a selection or numeric bound to the specification. |

| Pass condition | Every model clause is represented with no value merged, renamed, or dropped and no authority or provenance clause weakened. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any missing value, merged dimension, or diverging clause. Blocked: the status model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Matching value names while permitted-transition or authority clauses differ underneath. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-010 — Assignment-authority record-model audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's grant and authority-event records state every clause of the specification Section 9.6 assignment-authority record model, the grant-record identification of WP2-SCHED-017 among them. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-051 and WP2-SCHED-017, documentation half. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the grant and authority-event record definitions clause by clause against the record model. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A clause-by-clause conformance table for the grant record and every authority-event kind. |
| Pass condition | Every model clause is represented, correction events carrying both the current-predecessor and corrected-record references. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent clause, a correction model with one collapsed reference, or a reusable event identity. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A record model that names all fields while its correction semantics let the corrected record stand in for the predecessor. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-011 — Model-request register audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's model-request records state every clause of the specification Section 12.3 register for every record type, and that the three request layers of WP2-MODEL-053 — the request-specification binding, the semantic content identity or digest, and the per-attempt provider-facing rendering identity or digest — are three distinct recorded layers. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-MODEL-001 and WP2-MODEL-053, documentation half. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the request-record definitions clause by clause against the register and the three-layer model. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A clause-by-clause conformance table for all register record types and the three layers. |
| Pass condition | Every register clause is represented and the three layers are separately recorded, none derived from or standing in for another. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent clause or any two layers collapsed into one identity. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A model that stores three fields while populating two of them from one value. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-012 — Evidence-record register audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's evidence records state every clause of the specification Section 13.3 register for every one of its record types — identity scope, recording authority, required parent and source references, cardinality, point of existence, correction or supersession rule, and the rule on existence without execution evidence. |

| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the evidence-record definitions clause by clause against the register's introducing statements and entries. |

| Expected evidence | A clause-by-clause conformance table for all evidence record types. |

| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent or diverging clause. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |

**CHK-013 — Disposition-register and failure-taxonomy audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |

| Pass condition | Every register clause and every taxonomy class is represented distinctly, with multi-class events representable as separately named classes. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent clause, merged classes, or a classification that auto-derives consequences. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A taxonomy that lists all classes while its storage collapses them into one generic failure code. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's study-level records state every clause of the specification Section 15.2 completion-dimension model and the Section 15.3 study-record register — the seven dimensions with their recording authorities and unresolved-result meanings, and the ledger and aggregation record types with their cardinalities, points of existence, and correction rules. |

| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent clause or a combined completion scalar. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |

**CHK-015 — Concurrency-record binding audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's registered concurrency-profile and observed concurrency-provenance records state every clause of the specification Section 10.2 record and cardinality tables and carry every Section 10.5 category, with explicit not-applicable and not-observable representations, and that profile plus provenance suffice to reconstruct execution architecture and contention conditions at the categories' conceptual level without vendor or stack identity beyond what the ninth category itself requires. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-035 and WP2-SCHED-046, documentation half. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the two record definitions clause by clause and category by category. |

| Expected evidence | Clause-by-clause and category-by-category conformance tables. |
| Pass condition | Every clause and category is represented, absence states are explicit, and sufficiency holds without stack identity. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent category, implicit absence, or reconstruction that requires a vendor identity. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's timing records identify, for every timing fact, the time domain it belongs to — one of the seven specification Section 11.3 domains or a further named domain or stage never merged into one of the seven — and state every clause of the applicable domain entry. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-001, documentation half. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |

| Expected evidence | A domain-by-domain conformance table. |
| Pass condition | Every domain entry's clauses are represented and every further domain is named rather than folded into one of the seven. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any timing fact without a domain, any merged domain, or any diverging clause. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |

| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's credential-lifecycle records state every clause of the specification Section 16.6 credential-authority event model — event identity, operation identity and membership, credential concerned, lifecycle operation, predecessor and corrected-record references, governing policy, recording authority and reason, authority point, validity or usability fact, and correction rule. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-017, documentation half. |

| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the credential-event record definitions clause by clause against the event model. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A clause-by-clause conformance table for the event model. |
| Pass condition | Every model clause is represented, operation membership is explicit, and no record form carries secret material. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any absent clause, implicit membership, or a record form embedding secret material. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An event model that is complete for single-event operations while multi-event membership is reconstructed from timestamps. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-018 — Registered-policy identification audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every registered, versioned policy or design object the specification requires records to identify — scheduling, liveness, authority, authoritative-commit, retry and fallback, presentation-disposition, validation, budget, cancellation, replacement, materiality, evidence-contract, retention, secret-handling, and aggregation rules among them — is representable and identified by identity and version wherever a governed record names it, with explicit absence stated where none applies. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-009, WP2-SCHED-031, WP2-MODEL-055, WP2-FAIL-009, WP2-FAIL-010, WP2-EVID-059, WP2-SEC-010, WP2-AGG-022 — each for its policy-identification clause. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus; synthetic registered-study fixture for the policy inventory. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit every governed record definition for its policy identity-and-version fields and its explicit-absence representation. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A policy-by-policy audit table across record types. |
| Pass condition | Every required policy reference carries identity and version, and absence is explicit rather than inferred. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any unversioned, implicit, or absent-by-omission policy reference. Blocked: the record model is undocumented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Policy names recorded without versions, which only a policy revision would later expose. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-019 — Handoff-register audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a proposed implementation plan resolves the specification Section 18 handoff register as the `WP2-HAND` handoff-boundary requirements demand: every selected decision identified and versioned, every blocking decision resolved before acceptance, no requirement silently reinterpreted, no open question silently resolved, and every behavior-affecting choice identified under the charter's versioning and registration rules. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-018, WP2-HAND-019, WP2-HAND-021, WP2-HAND-022, WP2-HAND-023, WP2-HAND-024, WP2-HAND-033, WP2-HAND-035, WP2-HAND-042. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus, extended with the implementation plan under audit. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit the plan decision by decision against the register's canonical entries and coverage table. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | A decision-by-decision resolution table naming each register entry, its selected option, and its version consequence. |
| Pass condition | Every canonical register decision is addressed, every blocking one is resolved and versioned, and no resolution reinterprets a requirement. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an unaddressed blocking decision, an unversioned selection, or a resolution that amends a requirement. Blocked: no implementation plan exists — the check is not runnable before one is authorized. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A plan that names technologies without mapping them to register entries, leaving decisions resolved in appearance only. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-020 — Dependency and acceptance-gate audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the implementation's acceptance claims keep the three specification Section 20 gates distinct — documentation-package acceptance, implementation acceptance, and Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence — claiming none from another, and that every Section 19 dependency statement is respected: conformance treated as prerequisite rather than parity evidence, concurrency kept a recorded experimental factor, and no aggregate, rebuild, seal, or throughput result presented as parity. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-017, WP2-HAND-020, WP2-HAND-026, WP2-HAND-027, WP2-HAND-028, WP2-HAND-031, WP2-HAND-032, WP2-HAND-033, WP2-HAND-034, WP2-HAND-035, WP2-HAND-036, WP2-HAND-037, WP2-HAND-038. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit every acceptance claim and dependency statement in the assessment artifacts against the three gates. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record classifying every claim under exactly one gate. |
| Pass condition | Every claim names its own gate, and no gate is claimed, inferred, or implied from another. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any cross-gate inference or any parity claim from conformance artifacts. Blocked: acceptance artifacts do not yet exist. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Gate language kept distinct in formal statements while summary prose reports "accepted" without naming which gate. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

**CHK-021 — Open-questions register audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the specification Section 21 register discipline is respected at assessment time: no unresolved question that blocks implementation acceptance stands unresolved at an acceptance claim, no open question is used to weaken a requirement or defer a pass condition, and every later ruling carries explicit predecessor or supersession lineage. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-HAND-022, WP2-HAND-033, WP2-HAND-039, WP2-HAND-040, WP2-HAND-041, WP2-HAND-042, WP2-HAND-043. |
| Fixtures and setup | Record-audit corpus, extended with the Section 21 register state and the Section 7 `CQ` register state at assessment time. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Audit both registers, classify every entry's blocking effect, and trace every ruling's lineage. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean-path document audit. |
| Expected evidence | An entry-by-entry audit table with blocking classification and lineage. |
| Pass condition | No blocking entry stands unresolved against a claimed acceptance, and every ruling names what it rules on. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a blocking entry outstanding at acceptance, a silently rewritten entry, or a ruling without lineage. Blocked: register state is unavailable. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An entry reworded so that its blocking character is no longer visible, audited only by current status. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Static document audit; static. |

#### 2.2.2 Deterministic keyless execution checks (CHK-022 through CHK-067)

The checks of this class drive a candidate implementation through controlled, reproducible operations that depend on no live provider, no live credential, and no ordinary timing luck — clean paths, controlled adversarial operations, and the deterministic fault injections that need no forced interleaving of competing operations at an authority boundary. Where an entry of this class binds the scripted fake-provider fixture or the crash-and-restart harness, it does so only for a deterministic stimulus its own entry names — a scripted response, delay, or boundary omission, or a declared clock step — never for the provider-behavior accounting the class of Section 2.2.5 carries or the recovery paths the class of Section 2.2.4 carries. They carry the per-contract dynamic verification of the identity, isolation, lifecycle, scheduling, timing, secret-handling, and study-accounting contracts.

**CHK-022 — Planned-run-before-execution check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that planned-run identity exists and is recorded before, and independently of, any physical execution, that every execution attempt names exactly one planned-run parent, and that a planned run cancelled before any attempt admission remains a completely represented planned run of its study. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-006, WP2-ID-007, WP2-ID-008, WP2-LIFE-005, WP2-LIFE-008, WP2-EVID-009. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Register planned runs; admit and execute an attempt for one; cancel another before any admission; read the identity and lifecycle records of both. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled pre-admission cancellation. |
| Expected evidence | Planned-run identity records predating any admission; an attempt identity naming exactly one parent; the cancelled run's complete identity with zero attempts, no run evidence root, and no authoritative outcome. |
| Pass condition | Every planned-run identity is recorded before execution, every attempt names exactly one parent, and the never-executed run remains completely represented with zero attempts and no root. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a planned-run identity first appearing at execution, an attempt naming zero or two parents, or the cancelled run losing representation or acquiring a root. Blocked: registration cannot be established. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Identity records regenerated at query time rather than read from what was persisted at registration. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the cancelled run's representation is never cleaned up. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-023 — Identity uniqueness and duplicate-identity refusal check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every assigned identity distinguishes its instance throughout its register-stated scope across concurrent studies and attempts, that no identity or identity-and-version pair carries two meanings, that terminal or superseded instances never free their identities for reuse, and that a duplicate identity is refused with the typed, evidence-preserving refusal record. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-001, WP2-ID-003, WP2-ID-005, WP2-ID-024, WP2-ISO-030, WP2-ISO-031, WP2-ISO-032. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; two-study fixture for cross-study scope. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Create entity instances across both studies; complete and cancel some; then present operations that reuse a completed instance's identity and operations that assign one identity two meanings. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-001 — duplicate run identity. |
| Expected evidence | Distinct identities across the full scope; refusal records identifying the duplicate-identity condition and recording demonstrated lineage; pre-existing records intact. |
| Pass condition | Every duplicate presentation is refused with a distinguishable refusal naming the condition, and no identity is reused or re-meant. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a silently accepted duplicate, a freed identity, or a refusal that destroyed evidence. Blocked: the fixture cannot create the terminal instances the reuse attempts need. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A collision that storage-level path uniqueness happens to mask, leaving the identity-scope rule itself untested. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; refusal records and both colliding proposals preserved. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-024 — Recorded-lineage and reference check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every required parent or source reference is recorded with the identity record it belongs to, that version-required references carry version with identity, that locally scoped identifiers appear only within disambiguating persisted namespaces, and that every required lineage relationship and join is recorded explicitly at establishment, readable from the record rather than reconstructed from ordering, paths, names, or timing. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-002, WP2-ID-004, WP2-ID-022, WP2-ID-023, WP2-MODEL-023, WP2-SCHED-045. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute attempts end to end; read every created identity record and join record; then rename storage locations and permute record orderings in a copy and re-read the lineage. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled relocation and reordering probe. |
| Expected evidence | Identity records carrying every required reference and version; join records timestamped at establishment; lineage that resolves identically after relocation and reordering. |
| Pass condition | Every required reference and join is present, explicit, and stable under the relocation and reordering probe. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any reference reconstructed from path, order, name, or wall clock, or any version-less required reference. Blocked: the probe copy cannot be constructed. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Lineage read through a query layer that silently falls back to path and timestamp joins when explicit records are absent. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; probe copies are conformance evidence, originals untouched. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-025 — Correction and supersession discipline check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a material identity error is corrected only by a new identity or version naming what it supersedes, that a correction to a transition, status value, lineage-relation record, or disposition is a new record explicitly naming and superseding its target, and that nothing is re-meant or rewritten in place. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-025, WP2-ID-026, WP2-ID-027, WP2-LIFE-016, WP2-LIFE-017, WP2-LIFE-032, WP2-FAIL-001, WP2-FAIL-013, WP2-FAIL-014. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Introduce recorded errors under controlled conditions; drive the correction paths; separately present in-place mutation operations and verify their refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled correction and attempted in-place mutation operations. |
| Expected evidence | Correction records with explicit supersession lineage, correcting authority, and reason; the corrected originals intact; refusals of the in-place mutations. |
| Pass condition | Every correction is a new explicitly linked record, every original survives unchanged, and every in-place mutation is refused. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any in-place rewrite, any correction without named target, or a correction recorded as an ordinary successor transition. Blocked: the error conditions cannot be established. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A store that presents a corrected view by default, hiding that the underlying original was rewritten. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; corrected and correcting records both preserved. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-026 — Execution-agency identity distinctness check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that laboratory-worker, lease, and dispatch-wave identities are recorded as identities distinct from planned-run and execution-attempt identities, never joined or reported as one of them, and that reassigning the laboratory worker, lease, wave, or host of an attempt changes neither its planned run's identity nor its registered treatment. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-013, WP2-ID-014, WP2-ID-015. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Admit attempts under named laboratory workers, leases, and waves; read every record joining them; where the registered policy permits, drive host and lease changes and re-read run identity and treatment. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path. |
| Expected evidence | Four separately recorded identity kinds; joins by explicit reference only; unchanged planned-run identity and treatment across agency changes. |
| Pass condition | No agency identity is used as, or substituted for, a run or attempt identity, and agency changes leave run identity and treatment untouched. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any conflated identity or any treatment or identity change riding on an agency change. Blocked: the fixture cannot vary agency assignments. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Reports that display attempt identifiers derived from laboratory-worker names, conflating the identities only at the reporting join. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-027 — Evidence and derived-result identity check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every run evidence root belongs to exactly one attempt, that no storage location serves as the sole identity of a run or study evidence root, that a study evidence root is an identity-bearing namespace or registered set, that every sealed package identifies its boundary and inventory, and that every aggregation identifies its study, analysis version, and exact source set. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-017, WP2-ID-018, WP2-ID-019, WP2-ID-020, WP2-ID-021, WP2-EVID-010. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute attempts to sealed, aggregated completion; read root, package, and aggregation identity records; relocate a copy of the storage and verify identities survive location change. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled relocation probe. |
| Expected evidence | Root identities recorded separately from locations; package records naming boundary and inventory; aggregation records naming study, analysis version, and exact sources. |
| Pass condition | Every identity is explicit, location-independent, and single-owner where the register so states. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a root identified only by path, a package without its boundary, or an aggregation without its exact source set. Blocked: sealing or aggregation cannot complete in the fixture. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Identities that exist as fields but are populated by writing the storage path into them. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-028 — Output-root collision refusal check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a location the laboratory did not itself create or reserve under the exact expected evidence-root identity, empty or non-empty, is treated as an existing-root collision, never adopted or written into; that same-transaction creation is not treated as a collision; and that resume and recovery verify exact identity and provenance before acting on an existing location. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-026, WP2-ISO-027, WP2-ISO-028, WP2-ISO-029, WP2-EVID-011. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; prepared evidence corpus supplying pre-existing empty and non-empty locations. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Present admissions whose expected root location already exists in each provenance state; drive a legitimate same-transaction creation; drive resume operations against verified and unverifiable locations. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-002 — output-root collision. |
| Expected evidence | Collision refusals for both empty and non-empty unverified locations; clean adoption of the same-transaction creation; resume proceeding only after recorded identity-and-provenance verification. |
| Pass condition | Every unverified pre-existing location is refused, the legitimate creation is not, and no resume acts before verification. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: adoption of any unverified location — an empty one above all — or a resume acting on unverified evidence. Blocked: pre-existing locations cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Testing only non-empty collisions, passing an implementation that adopts empty directories on the assumption that empty means safe. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; refused locations preserved exactly as found. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-029 — Typed-refusal completeness check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that each of the seven WP2-ISO-030 conditions, presented separately, is refused rather than proceeded on; that every refusal is recorded as a distinguishable refusal identifying each condition met and such owning lineage as was demonstrated, stating that none was demonstrated where none was; and that no refusal deletes, overwrites, truncates, or renders unverifiable pre-existing or already-produced evidence. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-030, WP2-ISO-031, WP2-ISO-032. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; prepared evidence corpus for the collision and ownership-conflict conditions. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Present seven controlled operations, one per condition — duplicate identity, namespace collision, existing-root adoption, root-ownership conflict, stale-lease authority exercise, cross-lineage attribution, and lineage-less mutation — plus one operation meeting two conditions at once. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-002 — output-root collision, for the existing-root condition; the remaining conditions are controlled adversarial operations. |
| Expected evidence | Seven refusal records, each naming its condition and demonstrated lineage; the two-condition refusal naming both; every prior record intact. |
| Pass condition | All seven conditions refuse with correctly typed, lineage-honest, evidence-preserving records. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any condition proceeding, a refusal missing a met condition, an asserted lineage that was never demonstrated, or lost evidence. Blocked: a condition cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A generic error path that halts the operation without producing the typed refusal record, indistinguishable from success by absence. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; refusal records and the refused operations' inputs preserved. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-030 — Configuration and shared-asset resolution check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every treatment-contributing configuration value resolves from the unit's own registered configuration and is recorded with its evidence, that no ambient value determines a treatment-bearing property unrecorded, and that an asset contributing to treatment, configuration, world state, stochastic inputs, or evidence is shared only content-addressed or version-pinned, read-only for the duration, and recorded in each sharing unit's evidence by exact identity or digest. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-008, WP2-ISO-009, WP2-ISO-010. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; a version-pinned shared asset staged for both studies. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run units with differing registered configurations under identical ambient environments, and identical registered configurations under differing ambient environments; share the pinned asset; read each unit's evidence. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled ambient variation. |
| Expected evidence | Per-unit configuration records matching registered values regardless of ambient state; shared-asset references by digest in every sharing unit's evidence. |
| Pass condition | Treatment-bearing values track registered configuration only, ambient influence is absent or recorded against the unit, and asset sharing meets all three sharing conditions. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an ambient value reaching a treatment-bearing property unrecorded, or a shared asset that is mutable, unpinned, or unrecorded. Blocked: ambient variation cannot be controlled. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Ambient values that happen to equal registered values in the fixture, hiding the resolution order. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-031 — Stochastic-state and paired-input check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that mutable random-generator and stochastic-event state is never shared between, inherited by, or carried over into isolation units, and that a registered common-random-number or other paired design fixes its identity scheme, derivation rule, master inputs, and branch-conditional rule before execution, with every realized paired input identified in each consuming unit's evidence with event-level lineage. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-011, WP2-ISO-012. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture with a registered paired-input design in one study and independent stochastic state in the other. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute paired and unpaired units; read stochastic-state provenance and paired-input lineage; verify by rerun that pairing is reproducible from the registered scheme alone. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path. |
| Expected evidence | Per-unit stochastic state with no shared mutable lineage; registered derivation records predating execution; event-level pairing lineage or explicit absent-counterpart records. |
| Pass condition | No mutable stochastic state crosses units, and every pairing is reproducible from registered, pre-execution records. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: shared or inherited generator state, or pairing reconstructed from execution rather than registration. Blocked: the paired design cannot be registered in the fixture. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Two units seeded identically by coincidence of defaults, mimicking a registered pairing that was never fixed. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-032 — World-clock and event-stream isolation check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the logical clock and authoritative event stream of each independent simulated world remain isolated from every other world's, and that wall-clock order is never used as an implicit causal relation, ordering guarantee, or identity join between isolation units. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-013, WP2-ISO-014. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture running two independent worlds. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run two worlds with deliberately skewed and overlapping wall-clock activity; read each world's event stream, clock lineage, and every cross-unit join in the records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled wall-clock skew. |
| Expected evidence | Two disjoint event streams and logical clocks; every cross-unit join carried by explicit identity, none by time. |
| Pass condition | No event, tick, or join of one world references or depends on the other's clock or stream, and no recorded join rests on wall-clock order. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any shared clock state, cross-world event reference, or time-based join. Blocked: two worlds cannot run in the fixture. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Worlds that never ran close enough in wall-clock time for an implicit temporal join to fire. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-033 — Log and diagnostic attribution check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every retained log or diagnostic record carries explicit attribution to each Section 6 register entity whose activity it describes, that no record overwrites another unit's record or enters a shared stream without per-record attribution, and that no record is admitted as evidence of a unit it does not describe. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ISO-017, WP2-ISO-018. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture with interleaved logging into a shared diagnostic service. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run units whose logging interleaves in one shared stream; read per-record attribution; attempt admission of one unit's record as another's evidence. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled misadmission attempt. |
| Expected evidence | Per-record attribution naming every described unit and non-unit entity; a refusal of the cross-unit admission. |
| Pass condition | Every retained record is fully attributed and the misadmission is refused. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an unattributed shared-stream record, an overwrite, or an accepted misadmission. Blocked: shared-stream logging cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Attribution recovered from log-line prefixes parsed at read time rather than recorded attribution. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-034 — Status-dimension and transition-provenance check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the five scalar status dimensions are separately recorded and the lineage-relation collection separately kept append-only, none combined or inferred from another; that every transition names its full WP2-LIFE-013 content including predecessor linkage; and that complete transition history is reconstructible from the records with predecessor linkage read, not inferred from order, and nothing overwritten or re-meant. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-003, WP2-LIFE-004, WP2-LIFE-013, WP2-LIFE-015, WP2-LIFE-016. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive attempts through rich lifecycles; read every dimension separately; reconstruct history from records alone; permute record retrieval order and reconstruct again. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a retrieval-order permutation probe. |
| Expected evidence | Five separately recorded dimensions plus the collection; transition records complete in every named element; identical reconstruction under permuted retrieval. |
| Pass condition | Dimensions are independent, transitions complete, and history reconstruction is order-independent and lossless. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a combined or inferred dimension value, an incomplete transition record, or reconstruction that changes under permutation. Blocked: rich lifecycles cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Histories that reconstruct correctly only because insertion order coincided with logical order in the fixture. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-035 — Model-conformant transition check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every recorded planned-run and execution-attempt value and transition conforms to the applicable specification Section 8.5 or 8.6 model entry in every governed status dimension — entry condition, permitted predecessors and successors, terminality, permitted recording authority, outcome effect, and minimum provenance — and that recorded values use the models' state and disposition names. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-001, WP2-LIFE-014; the dynamic half of WP2-LIFE-002. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive attempts and planned runs through every reachable model value in the fixture; read each transition against the applicable model entry clause by clause. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path; illegal stimuli belong to CHK-036. |
| Expected evidence | Transition records whose values, authorities, and provenance match the model entries for every traversed value. |
| Pass condition | Every governed value of both applicable model entries is traversed at least once in the fixture, every recorded transition satisfies every clause of its model entry, and only model-named values appear. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any off-model value, authority, predecessor, or missing provenance element. Blocked: any governed value of an applicable model entry the fixture cannot reach — recorded per unreachable value with its cause, never pooled into a pass. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Traversing only the happy path so that rarely used values — pre-release cancellation among them — go unexercised while counted as covered. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-036 — Illegal-transition refusal check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a transition the model does not permit, a transition by an unpermitted authority, a transition that cannot demonstrate its predecessor revision or initial-recording status, and a transition without demonstrable minimum provenance are each refused; that each refusal is recorded distinguishably with the conditions met and demonstrated provenance; and that refusals preserve all prior records and evidence. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-029, WP2-LIFE-030, WP2-LIFE-031. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Present each refusal condition separately as a controlled operation, plus one operation meeting two conditions. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled illegal-transition operations; no environmental fault is injected. |
| Expected evidence | One distinguishable refusal record per condition, naming entity, attempted transition, every met condition, and demonstrated provenance; prior state intact. |
| Pass condition | Every illegal transition refuses with a complete typed record and no state or evidence loss. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an accepted illegal transition, an untyped halt, or a refusal that changed recorded status. Blocked: a condition cannot be presented. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Refusals implemented as silent no-ops, leaving the status unchanged but producing no distinguishable refusal record. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-037 — Admission-distinctness and pre-start terminality check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that admission is never recorded or counted as release, start, running, completion, finalization, or acceptance; that an attempt owns its run evidence root from admission; that an attempt cancelled or failed before execution activity remains fully represented with identity, root, owning laboratory worker, history, terminal disposition, and produced evidence; and that terminal representations are never erased or rendered unreadable. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-009, WP2-LIFE-010, WP2-LIFE-011, WP2-LIFE-012. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Admit attempts; cancel one and fail one before any execution activity; read every status surface, count, and report for admission-conflating language; attempt post-terminal erasure through ordinary cleanup paths. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled pre-start terminality. |
| Expected evidence | Admission events distinct from all later events; roots owned from admission; complete pre-start terminal representations; cleanup refusing to touch them. |
| Pass condition | All admission distinctions hold and pre-start terminal attempts remain completely represented and unerasable. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any conflated event, a root created only at start, or a lost pre-start attempt. Blocked: pre-start terminality cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Reporting surfaces that filter out zero-activity attempts, making their preservation impossible to confirm from reports alone. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the pre-start terminal attempts are themselves the preserved artifacts. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-038 — Authoritative-outcome linkage check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that successful finalization never by itself becomes authoritative acceptance; that an Authoritatively-accepted planned run names exactly one attempt whose finalization state is Finalization succeeded; that per-attempt finalization state is kept separate rather than shared; that the outcome record names either no attempt or exactly one; and that a never-accepted planned run remains fully represented with all attempts and dispositions. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-006, WP2-LIFE-007, WP2-LIFE-008, WP2-ID-009, WP2-ISO-019, WP2-SCHED-027. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Finalize attempts successfully without acceptance; accept one planned run's attempt through the authoritative-commit path; leave another run finalized but never accepted; read outcome and finalization records of all. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path. |
| Expected evidence | Finalized-but-unaccepted attempts with no outcome effect; one outcome record naming exactly one Finalization-succeeded attempt; separate per-attempt finalization state throughout. |
| Pass condition | Acceptance arrives only through the acceptance operation, names exactly one qualifying attempt, and never leaks from finalization. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: auto-acceptance on finalization, an outcome naming zero-or-two attempts as accepted, or shared mutable finalization state. Blocked: the commit path cannot run. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A reporting join that displays the newest successful finalization as "the outcome", masking the missing acceptance record. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-039 — Resume and further-attempt check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a resume by itself admits nothing and changes no status or outcome; that any further attempt of a resumed or crashed planned run arrives only under the applicable registered replacement or continuation authority as a new attempt identity with its own root; that further physical executions are never recorded as retries, restarts, resumes, or continuations; that admission decisions record reason, policy identity and version, and authority; and that further attempts change no earlier attempt's records and never count as new sample units. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-024, WP2-LIFE-025, WP2-SCHED-007, WP2-SCHED-008, WP2-FAIL-028, WP2-FAIL-029, WP2-FAIL-030, WP2-FAIL-031. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Resume planned runs with and without registered continuation authority; admit further attempts; read admission records, earlier-attempt records, and denominator surfaces. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled unauthorized-further-attempt operation. |
