| Expected evidence | Resume records with no status side effects; new attempt identities with own roots under named policies; unchanged earlier attempts; unchanged registered denominators. |
| Pass condition | Every further attempt is new, authorized, recorded with reason and policy, side-effect-free on predecessors, and denominators hold. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a resume that admits or mutates, an unauthorized admission proceeding, or a further attempt recorded as a continuation. Blocked: resume paths cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Conflating the fixture's resume with recovery so that the no-side-effect property is tested against the wrong operation. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-040 — Replacement lineage and non-erasure check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a registered replacement is admitted only under a preregistered replacement policy with typed reason and eligibility; that authorization and admission are distinct one-directional records, the authorization creating no attempt identity, root, or lineage record; that the replacement is an additional attempt of the same planned run whose lineage names the exact replaced attempt from the record, not from ordering or naming; that the replaced attempt and its evidence survive unchanged; and that eligibility, authorization, admission, execution, and acceptance remain five distinct decisions. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-ID-010, WP2-ID-011, WP2-ID-012, WP2-LIFE-026, WP2-FAIL-032, WP2-FAIL-033, WP2-FAIL-034, WP2-FAIL-036, WP2-FAIL-045. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a preregistered replacement policy; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive the full replacement chain for a failed attempt; drive a second replacement replacing a replacement; inject a replacement path that erases or renames its predecessor and verify detection. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-020 — replacement that erases the original attempt. |
| Expected evidence | Five distinct decision records; authorization naming proposed facts but no created identities; admission naming its authorization; lineage naming the exact predecessor; the predecessor byte-intact; a typed detection of the erasing variant. |
| Pass condition | The clean chain satisfies every naming and direction rule and the erasing variant is detected and refused rather than absorbed. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: erasure or renaming of the replaced attempt, lineage established by suffix or order, or an authorization that pre-creates identities. Blocked: the replacement policy cannot be preregistered in the fixture. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Verifying lineage by identifier convention — replacement suffixes among them — which passes exactly the implementations that violate WP2-ID-012. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the erased-predecessor variant's before-state is preserved for comparison. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-041 — Cancellation scope and cleanup preservation check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every cancellation is recorded at exactly one scope with authority, policy identity and version, reason, and affected units; that no cancellation is recorded at another scope or propagated to units its lineage does not place below it; that cancellation and cleanup act only within the owning unit and its recorded descendants; and that cancellation, cleanup, and post-commit cleanup never delete, truncate, overwrite, or render unverifiable sibling evidence, earlier attempts, terminal units, transition histories, authority records, accounting records, or exposure representations. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-027, WP2-LIFE-028, WP2-ISO-023, WP2-ISO-024, WP2-SCHED-034, WP2-FAIL-012, WP2-FAIL-022, WP2-FAIL-023, WP2-FAIL-025. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture with sibling runs and attempts in mixed states. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Issue cancellations at study, planned-run, and attempt scope; run cleanup after cancellation, failure, and commit; read affected-unit records and byte-compare sibling and terminal evidence before and after. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path across all three scopes with sibling exposure. |
| Expected evidence | Scope-typed cancellation records naming every changed unit; cleanup logs confined to owned lineage; identical before-and-after evidence for every sibling and terminal unit. |
| Pass condition | Every cancellation is correctly scoped and attributed and no out-of-lineage or preservation-protected record is altered. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: cross-scope recording, sibling disposition, or any lost or altered protected record. Blocked: sibling topology cannot be established. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Verifying preservation by record counts rather than content identity, missing truncation and overwrite in place. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; this check's subject is the cleanup discipline itself. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-042 — Behavioral-outcome misclassification check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a behaviorally unfavorable, repetitive, incoherent, or otherwise poor outcome is recorded as an observed research outcome, attributed and preserved like any other; that it is never treated by itself as operational failure, artifact invalidity, replacement eligibility, or rerun authorization; and that no recorded class by itself derives membership in another class or any lifecycle, evidence, acceptance, or reporting consequence. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-FAIL-006, WP2-FAIL-007, WP2-FAIL-008, WP2-FAIL-035. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture scripted to produce a behaviorally poor trajectory. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce the poor trajectory; then present replacement-eligibility, rerun, and invalidity operations grounded only on that outcome and verify their refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-044 — behaviorally unfavorable output misclassified as retry or replacement eligibility. |
| Expected evidence | The outcome recorded and preserved as an observed research outcome; refusals of every consequence operation grounded on it alone; no derived class memberships. |
| Pass condition | The outcome is preserved as evidence and grounds nothing by itself. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any consequence — eligibility above all — derived from the behavioral class alone. Blocked: a poor trajectory cannot be scripted. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A policy that formally requires a typed reason while accepting "poor output" as one, laundering the prohibited ground through a permitted field. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the unfavorable outcome is preserved unmodified. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-043 — Five-fact and materiality discipline check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that cause, observed class, lifecycle consequence, evidence consequence, and research disposition are five separately recorded facts, never collapsed; that multi-record events name their shared triggering identity; that every drawn consequence flows through an explicit disposition record naming its policy or stating absence; and that materiality classifications name a registered materiality policy identity and version never adopted or applied retroactively after observing favorability. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-FAIL-002, WP2-FAIL-003, WP2-FAIL-004, WP2-FAIL-009, WP2-FAIL-010, WP2-FAIL-011. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a registered materiality policy; deterministic engine fixture producing classified incidents. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce incidents spanning several register types; read the five facts, trigger linkage, and disposition records; present a materiality reclassification grounded on observed favorability and verify refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled incident production and a controlled retroactive-materiality operation. |
| Expected evidence | Five identifiable facts per incident; shared trigger identities across records; policy-named dispositions; refusal of the retroactive reclassification. |
| Pass condition | All separations hold, consequences flow only through dispositions, and materiality is fixed prospectively by the named policy. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a combined status word, a consequence without a disposition record, or an accepted favorability-driven reclassification. Blocked: multi-record incidents cannot be produced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Five stored fields populated by one classifier that writes the same value into several, separated in schema but not in fact. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-044 — Seven-operation distinctness check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the seven operations of specification Section 14.6 — transport or delivery retry, further provider attempt under registered policy, process restart, recovery over already-produced evidence, resume under verified identity, admission of a new attempt, and registered replacement — are recorded, reported, counted, and dispositioned as seven distinct operations, none interchangeable, extending WP2-SCOPE-006's recording distinctions. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCOPE-006, WP2-FAIL-016, WP2-FAIL-017, WP2-FAIL-018, WP2-FAIL-019, WP2-FAIL-020, WP2-FAIL-021. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture for the two retry kinds; crash-and-restart harness for the restart member. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive each of the seven operations at least once in one study; read every record, report, and count that touches them; verify no interrupted execution was continued in place. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path across all seven operations. |
| Expected evidence | Seven operation kinds distinctly recorded and counted; restart records with no status side effects; recovery confined to evidence and packaging work under verified identity. |
| Pass condition | Each operation is recorded as itself, none as another, and every distinction of the mapped requirements holds. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any two operations merged in records, reports, or counts, or an in-place continuation. Blocked: any of the seven cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Distinct record types written by paths that reuse one underlying counter, keeping kinds distinct but counts interchangeable. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-045 — Failure-containment check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the failure of one execution attempt is never recorded, propagated, or reported as, by itself, the failure or invalidity of its planned run, its study, or any sibling unit; that the failure of one planned run never by itself establishes study invalidity; and that failure handling erases no sibling evidence, terminates no unrelated isolation unit, merges no budget or evidence lineage, and causes no unit's result to be adopted by another. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-FAIL-015, WP2-FAIL-037, WP2-FAIL-038. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture with sibling runs and attempts. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Fail one attempt and, separately, exhaust one planned run's attempts in failure; run the failure-handling paths; read every status, disposition, and reporting surface of the parents and siblings and byte-compare sibling evidence and lineage across the handling. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled deterministic failures. |
| Expected evidence | Failure records confined to the failing units; parent and sibling statuses, evidence, budgets, and lineage unchanged except through explicit recorded dispositions. |
| Pass condition | No parent or sibling acquires a failure, invalidity, or degraded status from the failures alone, and failure handling leaves sibling evidence, units, lineage, and results untouched. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any implicit propagation upward or sideways, or any sibling erasure, termination, merge, or adoption during handling. Blocked: sibling topology cannot be established. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Propagation that occurs only in derived reporting views, which a records-only read would miss. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-046 — Study-invalidity decision check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that study invalidity is recorded only as the explicit, versioned, evidence-linked decision of the specification Section 14.2 register, decided by the registered authority; that the decision record identifies its evidence, rule identity and version, claim boundary, and diagnostic-continuation ruling; that an invalid study remains represented and auditable; that no invalidity decision authorizes evidence deletion, history erasure, or favorable-result reruns; and that operational closure and invalidity remain co-representable, neither overriding the other. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-FAIL-039, WP2-FAIL-042, WP2-FAIL-043, WP2-FAIL-044, WP2-AGG-042, WP2-AGG-043. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with registered validity rules; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive a study to operational closure; record an invalidity decision; then present evidence-deletion and further-admission operations grounded on the invalidity, and an operational-success operation purporting to override it. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled invalidity decision and controlled adversarial follow-on operations. |
| Expected evidence | A complete invalidity decision record; the study's membership, dispositions, and evidence intact; refusals of the deletion, rerun, and override operations; both dimension results current. |
| Pass condition | Invalidity arrives only through the register's decision record, authorizes nothing destructive, and coexists with operational closure. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: invalidity inferred from failures, any authorized destruction, or either dimension suppressing the other. Blocked: validity rules cannot be registered in the fixture. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An invalidity flag stored on the study object rather than a decision record, indistinguishable on happy-path reads. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the invalid study is preserved whole. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-047 — Scheduling-event and liveness-evidence check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that queue presence, delivery, assignment, grant of assignment authority, execution admission, release, execution start, and authoritative acceptance are recorded as distinct, separately attributable events; that neither queue presence, delivery, dequeuing, nor liveness evidence is treated as execution or transition authority; that absence of liveness evidence establishes death, failure, or safe reassignment only as the registered liveness policy provides; and that every liveness determination records policy identity and version, the evidence and absence relied on, the determining authority, and each changed entity. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-001, WP2-SCHED-002, WP2-SCHED-018, WP2-SCHED-019, WP2-SCHED-020. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a registered liveness policy. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive units through the full scheduling event sequence; withhold and supply liveness evidence under the registered policy; present execution operations grounded only on delivery or heartbeat and verify refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled liveness-evidence withholding. |
| Expected evidence | Eight distinct event kinds with per-event attribution; refusals of authority-from-delivery and authority-from-heartbeat operations; complete liveness determination records. |
| Pass condition | Every event is distinct, no non-authority fact confers authority, and every determination is policy-bound and fully recorded. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: merged events, authority inferred from delivery or liveness, or a determination without its policy and evidence basis. Blocked: liveness evidence cannot be controlled. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Events recorded distinctly while an internal fast path executes directly from dequeue, bypassing the recorded grant. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-048 — Assignment and dispatch-wave record check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every admitted attempt names exactly one owning laboratory-worker identity from admission through terminality, never reassigned; and that every dispatch-wave record names every admitted member attempt — cancelled-before-release and never-started attempts among them — with each member naming the wave in its own record at membership establishment rather than deriving it afterwards. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-003, WP2-SCHED-004, WP2-SCHED-011, WP2-SCHED-012. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with dispatch waves containing released, cancelled, and never-started members. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Form waves; cancel one member before release; leave one member unstarted; present a reassignment operation; read wave and member records bidirectionally. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled reassignment operation. |
| Expected evidence | Owner identity stable across every attempt's whole lifecycle; wave records naming all members including the cancelled and unstarted; member records naming the wave from establishment; the reassignment refused. |
| Pass condition | Ownership is admission-fixed and wave membership is complete, bidirectional, and establishment-time recorded. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any reassignment, a wave omitting an unreleased member, or membership derived from ordering or naming. Blocked: waves cannot be formed. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Wave completeness checked against released members only, exactly the population where omission cannot occur. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-049 — Duplicate-delivery idempotency check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that repeated delivery of the same assignment for the same admitted attempt resolves idempotently — no further attempt identity, no further root, no second grant — and that a delivery, assignment, or wave-membership record conflicting with the recorded ones is refused with a distinguishable refusal naming the conflicting facts, never merged, reconciled, or partially applied. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-005, WP2-SCHED-006. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Deliver one assignment several times identically; then deliver conflicting assignment and wave-membership records for the same attempt; read attempt, root, grant, and refusal records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-009 — duplicate dispatch. |
| Expected evidence | One attempt identity, one root, one grant across all duplicate deliveries; typed refusals naming the conflicting facts for the conflicting variants. |
| Pass condition | Duplicates are absorbed idempotently and conflicts are refused whole. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any second identity, root, or grant, or a conflict merged or partially applied. Blocked: redelivery cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Idempotency achieved by overwrite — last delivery wins — which deduplicates identities while silently replacing recorded facts. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; all duplicate and conflicting deliveries preserved as received. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-050 — Simultaneous-attempt policy check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that whether one planned run may hold more than one nonterminal attempt is fixed by the registered, versioned scheduling policy its records identify, never by unrecorded infrastructure, delivery, or recovery behavior, and that plural current grants over one planned run exist only where that policy permits plural nonterminal attempts and each grant names a distinct admitted attempt. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-009, WP2-SCHED-010, WP2-SCHED-015. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture carrying one single-attempt policy and one plural-attempt policy. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Under each policy, present second-attempt admissions and second grants; read policy identification in the run records and the grant-to-attempt relations. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled admissions under both registered policies. |
| Expected evidence | Run records identifying their policy and version; refusal of the second nonterminal attempt under the single policy; distinct grant-per-attempt relations under the plural policy. |
| Pass condition | Simultaneity follows the named policy exactly, and plural grants map one-to-one onto distinct admitted attempts. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: simultaneity varying with infrastructure behavior, or two current grants naming one attempt. Blocked: both policies cannot be registered. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A fixture whose delivery path never produces the second admission attempt, leaving the policy bound but unexercised. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-051 — Grant-record and authority-event check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every grant, renewal, revocation, release, supersession, and correction is an individually attributable event with a never-reused identity naming its grant and predecessor event — an issuance naming its created grant and predecessor absence, a correction naming both its current predecessor and, distinctly, the corrected record; that every grant record carries the full WP2-SCHED-017 identification; that at most one grant is current per attempt at one logical authority point, currentness read from recorded authority facts rather than delivery, possession, or arrival; and that grant records conform to every record-model clause in operation. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-014, WP2-SCHED-016, WP2-SCHED-017; the dynamic half of WP2-SCHED-051. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive full grant lifecycles including renewal, revocation, release, supersession, and correction; read every event's linkage; probe currentness reads against reordered record retrieval. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path across all authority-event kinds. |
| Expected evidence | Complete event chains with explicit predecessor linkage; corrections carrying dual references; exactly one current grant per attempt at every probed point. |
| Pass condition | Every event is complete, linked, and non-reused, and currentness is record-derived and single-valued per attempt. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a reused event identity, a correction with one collapsed reference, or currentness inferred from possession or order. Blocked: an event kind cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Currentness computed correctly from records in the probe while the execution path consults an in-memory holder flag. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-052 — Acceptance-precondition and preserved-result check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that no attempt is considered for authoritative acceptance without recorded Completed operational state, Finalization-succeeded finalization state, and matching identity and lineage; that the acceptance operation verifies everything WP2-SCHED-026 enumerates before recording; that every acceptance record names both the current authoritative-commit authority and the accepted attempt's complete relevant assignment-authority history establishing unbroken succession from Starting to Finalization succeeded, ownership never conferring commit authority; and that losing, late, duplicate, stale, cancelled, and superseded results are preserved with distinguishable non-authoritative dispositions. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-025, WP2-SCHED-026, WP2-SCHED-029, WP2-SCHED-053. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with registered authoritative-commit policy; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive clean acceptances; present acceptance operations for non-Completed, non-finalized, mismatched-lineage, cancelled, and already-accepted subjects; deliver late and duplicate results; read acceptance and disposition records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled ineligible-acceptance operations; racing variants belong to CHK-073. |
| Expected evidence | Acceptance records with dual authority naming and complete succession histories; refusals for every ineligible subject naming the failed verification; preserved non-authoritative dispositions for every losing or late result. |
| Pass condition | Only fully verified subjects are accepted, every acceptance carries both authorities and the succession history, and nothing losing is lost. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an unverified acceptance, an acceptance without the succession history, or an undispositioned losing result. Blocked: the commit path cannot run. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A verification routine that reads the same cached status the acceptance writer maintains, verifying its own assumption rather than the records. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; losing results preserved with their dispositions. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-053 — Time-domain identification and non-inference check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every recorded timing fact identifies its time domain and conforms to the applicable domain entry in operation; that every timeout, expiry, freshness test, and stop condition identifies its evaluation domain; that no logical-tick deadline is inferred from a wall-clock duration; that no wall-clock timeout is recorded as logical expiry or supersession; and that no domain's interval is recorded as another's, every derived interval carrying its recorded derivation and sources. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-001, WP2-TIME-005, WP2-TIME-006, WP2-TIME-007, WP2-TIME-008. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture producing activity in every fixture-reachable domain. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute attempts producing timing facts across domains; apply wall-clock and logical-tick timeouts; read every timing record's domain identification and every derived interval's derivation record. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled timeouts in both domain kinds. |
| Expected evidence | Domain-identified timing facts conforming to their entries; domain-identified timeout evaluations; derived intervals naming sources, clocks, and derivation. |
| Pass condition | Every timing fact, timeout, and derivation is domain-explicit and no cross-domain inference or substitution appears. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an undomained fact, a wall-clock-derived logical expiry, or an interval presented across domains without derivation. Blocked: multi-domain activity cannot be produced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | One shared timeout mechanism labeled per call site, where labels assert domains the mechanism does not actually evaluate in. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-054 — Logical-time insulation check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that logical simulation time is engine-owned and advances only through the world's authoritative rules; that queueing, dispatch, transport, provider, execution, and finalization delays neither advance logical time, alter simulated psychology, create causal events, change decision eligibility, nor change expiry or supersession behavior; and that any intended delay effect exists only as a preregistered, engine-owned, explicitly recorded treatment or condition difference. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-002, WP2-TIME-003, WP2-TIME-004, WP2-MODEL-051. |
| Fixtures and setup | Deterministic engine fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture supplying controlled delays; synthetic registered-study fixture carrying one preregistered delay-effect treatment. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run paired executions differing only in injected non-logical delays; compare logical clocks, event streams, and decisions; run the preregistered delay treatment and read its engine-owned causation records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-013 — provider rate limiting, for the rate-limit-delay variant; the remaining non-logical delays are controlled stimuli rather than catalogued faults. |
| Expected evidence | Identical logical timelines and decisions across the delay-varied pair; the treatment variant's effect recorded as registered, engine-owned causation. |
| Pass condition | Non-logical delay is causally inert in every simulated world except through the preregistered treatment path. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any logical-time, psychology, eligibility, or expiry difference attributable to delay outside the registered treatment. Blocked: paired delay-varied executions cannot run. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Delays injected at points the engine never samples in this fixture, leaving sampled paths untested. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-055 — Stage-fact and unobservable-boundary check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that provider-accepted, provider-completed, local-response-received, response-validated, and simulation-accepted stages are separately identified stage facts, never one timestamp or interval; that an unobservable required boundary or interval is recorded as unobserved or unavailable with reason where known; that nothing infers it from neighbors, local totals, or other units; and that unobservable provider boundaries are so stated in the provider stage-fact record. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-009, WP2-TIME-010, WP2-TIME-011, WP2-MODEL-017. |
| Fixtures and setup | Scripted fake-provider fixture with observable and deliberately unobservable stage boundaries; synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run requests whose scripted provider omits acceptance and completion boundaries; read stage facts, absence statements, and any inferred values. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled boundary omission by script. |
| Expected evidence | Five separable stage facts where observable; explicit unobserved-or-unavailable records with reasons for the omitted boundaries; no inferred stand-ins. |
| Pass condition | Every stage is separate and every unobservable boundary is stated, never inferred. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: merged stage facts or an inferred boundary value. Blocked: boundary omission cannot be scripted. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An absent boundary silently defaulted to a neighboring timestamp at record-write time, appearing observed thereafter. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-056 — Duration-basis check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that laboratory-worker runtime, finalization time, and study-level elapsed time each name their exact start and end event identities; that every locally measured duration identifies a monotonic or equivalently safe basis shared by both boundary marks, an interval without one common basis taking the WP2-TIME-022 treatment instead; that human-readable instants are recorded separately from duration measurements; and that local queue time ends exactly at the recorded Released transition or earlier terminal transition, any release-to-start delay recorded as a separately named stage. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-012, WP2-TIME-013, WP2-TIME-014, WP2-TIME-021. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; crash-and-restart harness only to vary clock-adjustment exposure by controlled clock stepping in the fixture environment. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute attempts while stepping the wall clock between boundary marks; read every duration's basis, boundary event identities, instant records, and the queue-time end boundary. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled clock stepping as stimulus. |
| Expected evidence | Durations naming shared bases and exact boundary events, unaffected by the clock step; separate instant records; queue time bounded by the Released transition with any further delay separately named. |
| Pass condition | Every measured duration is basis-identified, boundary-identified, and step-immune, and queue-time boundaries are exact. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a duration spanning bases, computed from instants, or a queue time absorbing post-release delay. Blocked: clock stepping is unavailable in the venue. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A wall-clock subtraction relabeled monotonic in the record, exposed only under a clock step some venues cannot perform — in which case the outcome is blocked, not passed. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-057 — Cross-host timing provenance check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that timing records compared or joined across hosts carry clock source, host or runner association, material synchronization uncertainty, and the offset basis recorded against the comparison; that cross-host wall-clock order is never a causal relation, identity join, or tie-break; that a self-reported interval whose marks lack one common safe basis is recorded as unobserved or as an explicit derived estimate with full source, clock, offset, and uncertainty identification; and that no clock's observation substitutes for another's missing boundary mark or closes an interval its own basis leaves open. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-015, WP2-TIME-016, WP2-TIME-022, WP2-TIME-023. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture arranged across two runner associations with deliberately skewed clocks; scripted fake-provider fixture supplying provider-reported marks. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce cross-host comparisons and joins; present interval closures mixing local and provider marks; read comparison records, derived estimates, and substitution attempts. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled clock skew and controlled mixed-mark closure operations. |
| Expected evidence | Comparison records with per-record provenance and offset basis; derived estimates fully identified; refusal or unobserved treatment of every mixed-basis closure. |
| Pass condition | Every cross-host use is provenance-complete and no mixed-basis interval is reported as measured. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a time-based join or tie-break, or a provider mark closing a local interval. Blocked: two runner associations cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Skew too small in the fixture for substitution to produce visibly impossible values, hiding the substitution path. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-058 — Timing-integrity preservation and attribution check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that negative, discontinuous, contradictory, or impossible timing records are preserved exactly as recorded, never normalized, clamped, corrected, reordered, or discarded, and surfaced as distinguishable typed integrity problems attributed to their units and domains; and that every retained timing record is attributable to study, planned run, attempt, laboratory worker, request or deciding episode, and registered concurrency profile, naming each that applies and stating absences. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-TIME-017, WP2-TIME-018, WP2-TIME-019, WP2-TIME-020. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; controlled clock stepping to produce an impossible record. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce an impossible timing record by controlled clock manipulation; read its preservation, its typed surfacing, and the attribution chain of every retained timing record. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled production of an impossible record. |
| Expected evidence | The impossible record byte-preserved; a typed integrity problem attributed to unit and domain; complete attribution with explicit absences on every timing record. |
| Pass condition | Impossible records survive untouched and surfaced, and attribution is complete with explicit absences. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any normalization or silent discard, or an unattributed timing record. Blocked: an impossible record cannot be produced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A storage layer that rejects impossible values at write time, so the record is never persisted and its absence resembles cleanliness. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the impossible record is itself preserved evidence. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-059 — Secret-surface prohibition check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that live secret material appears on none of the fourteen separately prohibited surfaces of WP2-SEC-002, and that secret material never becomes part of logical-request content, the immutable request specification, or any evidence identity — digest, inventory entry, seal, or other identity-bearing value. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-002, WP2-SEC-004. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic credential fixture with detector-recognizable synthetic markers; deterministic engine fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture for request-path surfaces. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute attempts with the synthetic material present in the dispatch boundary; sweep every one of the fourteen surface kinds and every identity-bearing value for the marker. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path; the marker sweep is the observation. |
| Expected evidence | A surface-by-surface sweep report with zero marker occurrences outside the dispatch boundary. |
| Pass condition | The marker appears on no prohibited surface and in no identity-bearing value. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any marker occurrence on any of the fourteen surfaces or in any identity. Blocked: a surface kind cannot be produced in the fixture — recorded per surface, never pooled into a pass. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A sweep that recognizes the marker's literal form but not encoded, split, or transformed occurrences. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; sweep reports preserved with per-surface outcomes. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-060 — Credential and session isolation check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that credential material and raw provider-session state held for one study, planned run, attempt, laboratory worker, or provider session never becomes available to another through shared infrastructure; that secret material available to one isolation unit never reaches another through shared process, store, or connection; and that any one-credential-many-units use is confined to a versioned, identified provider-dispatch secret boundary that withholds the credential and raw session state from every served unit while preserving complete per-unit attribution. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-005, WP2-SEC-006, WP2-ISO-025. |
| Fixtures and setup | Two-study fixture; synthetic credential fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture behind a fixture provider-dispatch secret boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Serve both studies through the shared boundary; sweep every unit-accessible surface for the synthetic material; read per-unit request, usage, project-scope, and treatment attribution. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with shared-boundary service. |
| Expected evidence | Zero synthetic-material occurrences in any served unit's space; boundary identity and version recorded; complete per-unit attribution through the shared path. |
| Pass condition | The credential and session state stay inside the boundary and attribution survives sharing intact. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any cross-unit availability or attribution loss under sharing. Blocked: the shared boundary cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Isolation validated at API level while a shared connection object exposes raw session state to unit code. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-061 — Nonsecret-reference check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every nonsecret credential reference stays stable across every record the Sections 10 and 12 contracts join through it; that no reference reveals, contains, or permits reconstruction of the secret it names; and that issuance of a secret-derived reference and every mapping of a reference to secret material remain within the provider-dispatch secret boundary, the reference itself remaining usable outside. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-007, WP2-SEC-008, WP2-SEC-009. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic credential fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture; record-audit corpus for reference-form audit. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Execute joined request, usage, and provenance records through one reference; audit the reference's form against the synthetic material; drive a reference-resolution operation and locate where it executes. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path. |
| Expected evidence | One stable reference across all joined records; a form audit showing no derivation path back to the material; resolution records confined to the boundary. |
| Pass condition | References are stable, irreversibly nonsecret, and resolved only inside the boundary. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a reference that varies across joined records, embeds recoverable material, or resolves outside the boundary. Blocked: joined records cannot be produced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A truncated-secret reference that a form audit passes but that narrows reconstruction space — detectable only by derivation audit, not by inspection. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-062 — Credential-history and audit-record check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every rotation, revocation, or replacement is recorded through the nonsecret reference and version as credential-authority events naming recording authority and registered policy identity and version, with no secret in any record; that no such operation erases, rewrites, or reattributes historical attribution, every record keeping the reference and version current for it at its authority point; and that every access, denial, rotation, revocation, replacement, and boundary violation is auditable from secretless records carrying the WP2-SEC-012 identification. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-010, WP2-SEC-011, WP2-SEC-012. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic credential fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture; two credential versions with a controlled rotation between recorded uses. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Use version one; rotate; use version two; deny one access; read the event chain, historical attributions, and the audit trail; sweep all of it for the synthetic marker. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled rotation and denial; racing variants belong to CHK-078. |
| Expected evidence | Policy-named credential-authority events; pre-rotation records still attributed to version one; a denial record with its validity-fact basis; a marker-free audit trail. |
| Pass condition | History is version-faithful, events are complete and secretless, and the audit trail stands without the secret. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: reattributed history, a secret-bearing record, or an unauditable access or denial. Blocked: rotation cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Audit completeness checked against successful accesses only, leaving denials and violations unlogged. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-063 — Detected-secret refusal check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that candidate output in which live secret material is detected is refused ordinary persistence and admission as evidence; that every refusal produces a typed, secretless incident record with the full WP2-SEC-014 identification and explicit absences; that refusal never silently deletes, redacts, truncates, or renders unverifiable authoritative evidence, everything preservable without the secret remaining preserved; and that every sanitized derivative is explicitly identified as sanitized with nonsecret lineage to the incident record and, through it, the refused source event, retaining no secret material. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-013, WP2-SEC-014, WP2-SEC-015, WP2-SEC-016. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic credential fixture with detector-recognizable marker; scripted fake-provider fixture returning marker-bearing responses; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Script marker-bearing provider responses, derived artifacts, and log lines; drive persistence; produce a sanitized derivative; read incident records, preserved surroundings, and derivative lineage. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-034 — live secret detected before persistence. |
| Expected evidence | Refused persistence for every marker-bearing candidate; complete typed incident records without the marker; unaffected records intact; sanitized derivatives labeled with full nonsecret lineage. |
| Pass condition | Detection refuses persistence, incidents are complete and secretless, nothing else is lost, and derivatives are labeled and lineage-bound. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a persisted marker, a secret-bearing incident record, silent redaction of authoritative evidence, or an unlabeled derivative. Blocked: detection cannot be exercised on a surface. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A detector tuned to the fixture's marker format, passing while realistic secret shapes would flow through — the check verifies the refusal contract, and detector breadth stays a recorded limitation. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; incident records and derivatives preserved, the refused candidate withheld rather than stored. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-064 — Ledger derivation and missingness check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every study-membership-and-completion ledger derives from the admitted registration and recorded identities, lineage, and dispositions — never from discovered files, layout, ordering, or successful outcomes; that it includes every registered planned run including zero-attempt runs, every admitted attempt of every kind, exact attempt-to-run and replacement relations, every interpretation-required disposition, and every expected study-level record and root; that per-run it records zero-or-more attempts and at most one accepted outcome; that missingness reasons are exact without denominator removal; and that no undispositioned unit disappears from any surface for lack of evidence or success. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-008, WP2-AGG-009, WP2-AGG-013, WP2-AGG-014, WP2-AGG-017. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture populated with zero-attempt runs, replaced attempts, failures, cancellations, and one undispositioned unit; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Derive the ledger; delete a discovered-file decoy and permute storage in a copy; re-derive and compare; read membership, relations, missingness reasons, and the undispositioned unit's presence. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a storage-permutation probe. |
| Expected evidence | Identical ledgers across the probe; complete membership including zero-attempt and undispositioned units; exact missingness reasons; stable denominators. |
| Pass condition | The ledger is registration-derived, complete, relation-exact, and permutation-immune, with no vanishing units. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: file-discovery sensitivity, an omitted zero-attempt or undispositioned unit, or a missingness reason collapsed to a generic value. Blocked: the mixed population cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A ledger derived from records that were themselves populated by directory scanning one layer down. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-065 — Denominator reporting check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the registered sample denominator is reported as the ledger's count of registered planned runs and nothing else, that execution attempts are reported in a separate operational denominator never merged with or substituted for it, and that a registered replacement never increases the registered denominator. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-010, WP2-AGG-011, WP2-AGG-012. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with replacements and further attempts present. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Read every denominator-bearing report before and after admitting replacements and further attempts. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path. |
| Expected evidence | A registered denominator equal to registered planned runs throughout; a separate operational denominator absorbing all attempt growth. |
| Pass condition | Both denominators are correct, separate, and replacement-stable. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a merged, substituted, or replacement-inflated denominator. Blocked: replacements cannot be admitted. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Correct denominators in the ledger with a reporting layer that recomputes them from attempt counts. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-066 — Completion-dimension separation check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that the seven completion dimensions are separately recorded with explicit initial unresolved results recorded at ledger derivation as initializations; that no dimension is combined with, inferred from, or standing for another or for whole-study completion; that tension states — operationally closed yet unresolved elsewhere, fully dispositioned yet invalid — remain representable with every result current; that aggregation production resolves no other dimension; and that no dimension is declared reached merely because desired success counts exist. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-002, WP2-AGG-003, WP2-AGG-005, WP2-AGG-006, WP2-AGG-015; racing recordings belong to CHK-082. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture driving studies into both tension states. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Derive ledgers and read initial results; drive the tension states; produce an aggregate; present a success-count-based reached declaration and verify refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a controlled count-based declaration. |
| Expected evidence | Seven explicit initial results per study; both tension states current simultaneously; unchanged sibling dimensions after aggregation; the count-based declaration refused. |
| Pass condition | Dimensions are independent, initialized explicitly, tension-capable, and immune to success-count shortcuts. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an unresolved state read from record absence, an inferred dimension, or a count-based reach. Blocked: tension states cannot be driven. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A reporting rollup that renders seven stored results as one status word, reintroducing the scalar the records avoid. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

**CHK-067 — Zero-evidence study check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a study producing no execution-attempt evidence still receives its ledger, completion-dimension results, missingness records, and any registered invalidity decision, its ledger recording every registered planned run, every admitted attempt where any, and each run's exact no-outcome reason; and that no aggregation identity, empty aggregate, or purported derived combination is created merely to fill the absence. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-036, WP2-AGG-037. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture registered but never executed, and one variant with admitted never-started attempts. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive study-level accounting for both variants; read ledgers, dimension results, and missingness; present an empty-aggregation operation and verify refusal. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path over absent execution. |
| Expected evidence | Complete ledgers and dimension results over zero evidence; exact per-run reasons; refusal of the fictitious aggregation. |
| Pass condition | Zero-evidence studies are fully accounted and never decorated with fabricated aggregates. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a missing ledger or dimension result, a generic reason, or a created empty aggregate. Blocked: study-level accounting cannot run without execution. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Accounting paths that initialize only on first execution activity, leaving the zero-evidence study absent rather than accounted — absence misread as trivially clean. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Deterministic keyless execution; local or CI. |

#### 2.2.3 Concurrency and race checks (CHK-068 through CHK-086)

The checks of this class force or systematically enumerate the material interleavings at each authority boundary the specification makes race-safe — never relying on ordinary timing luck. The authority-boundary checks of the class verify, for every enumerated interleaving, that at most one competitor becomes current, that no partial or merged outcome exists, and that every loser is preserved as the distinguishable record the governing requirement states; the concurrency-exposure checks CHK-083, CHK-084, CHK-085, and CHK-086 verify instead that no unit's records, enforcement outcomes, or record joins differ from their isolated or mirrored baseline under forced contention. Each check runs keylessly under the controlled-interleaving harness.

**CHK-068 — All-or-none admission race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the admission boundary that two competing admissions, or two competing initial grant issuances, for one proposed attempt never produce a partially admitted attempt or an orphan current grant, the winner's attempt identity, root identity, owner relation, initial status recordings, initial collection state, and exactly one backing grant becoming authoritative together or not at all, with every losing, stale, conflicting, or incomplete admission preserved as a refusal or rejection carrying its proposed identities while creating nothing. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-013; the admission-boundary clauses of WP2-LIFE-009 and WP2-EVID-007. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the admission boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate the material interleavings of two admissions for one proposed attempt and of two initial issuances, including interruption between constituent records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-003 — two laboratory workers claiming one unit; FLT-025 — partial attempt admission. |
| Expected evidence | For every interleaving: one complete admitted attempt or none; no orphan grant; loser records with proposed identities; no partially present record set. |
| Pass condition | Every enumerated interleaving yields all-or-none admission with preserved losers. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any interleaving leaving a partial attempt, dual admission, or orphan grant. Blocked: the harness cannot reach the boundary's intermediate points. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Enumerating only whole-operation orderings while the violating interleavings interrupt between constituent writes. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; every loser and partial artifact preserved for inspection. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-069 — Transition-currency race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the transition authority boundary that at most one transition per current revision, one initial recording per dimension, and one correction per corrected record become current under every enumerated interleaving, currentness never inferred from arrival order, and that every losing or stale operation is preserved as a rejection naming entity, dimension, claimed predecessor or initial-or-correction character, attempted value, authority, and reason, destroying nothing. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-034, WP2-LIFE-035. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the transition boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate races between two transitions from one revision, two initial recordings of one dimension, two corrections of one record, and a stale-revision operation against an advanced dimension. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-024 — stale authority during a state transition. |
| Expected evidence | Single-current outcomes throughout; complete rejection records for every loser; identical results under reversed presentation orders. |
| Pass condition | Every race resolves to one current record with complete, preserved loser records, order-independently. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: dual currentness, a lost loser, or order-dependent resolution. Blocked: the boundary's races cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A serialized fixture path that never lets two operations reach the boundary concurrently, testing sequence rather than race. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-070 — Compound finalization-coherence race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the coherence decision that entry into Finalizing and entry into Finalization in progress become current together or not at all sharing one triggering identity; that resolution out of Finalizing accepts exactly one coherent compound outcome — Completed with its named Finalization-succeeded result, or Failed or Cancelled with the generic failed result; that no accepted success coexists with Failed or Cancelled; and that every unaccepted competing pair member is preserved supplying a current value in neither dimension. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-LIFE-033; the co-acceptance clauses of WP2-EVID-014 and WP2-EVID-037. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the coherence boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate races between a completion pair and a failure or cancellation pair, interruptions between the two entry transitions, and success recordings racing failure operations. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-026 — partial finalization or mismatched operational/finalization results. |
| Expected evidence | Paired entries current together or not at all; exactly one compound outcome per attempt per interleaving; preserved rejected pairs; closure records co-accepted with the terminal value. |
| Pass condition | Every interleaving yields one coherent compound outcome with both dimensions explicit and losers preserved. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a half-current pair, success beside Failed or Cancelled, or a mismatched compound. Blocked: pair interruption cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Coherence maintained by one stored value rendered into two dimensions at read time — inference the mapped requirements prohibit — which racing writes would never expose. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-071 — Grant-operation race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the grant authority boundary that two operations succeeding one current authority event, two issuances for one proposed or admitted attempt, and two corrections of one grant record or event never both become current; that a correction prevails only while its named current predecessor remains current, the corrected record never standing in for it; and that every losing or stale grant operation is preserved as the fully named rejection WP2-SCHED-052 states, destroying no grant, event, status, or evidence. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-052, WP2-SCHED-014, WP2-SCHED-016. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the grant boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate renewal-versus-revocation races from one event, dual issuance races, dual correction races, and a correction racing the advancement of its predecessor. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-023 — competing initial authority or credential issuance. |
| Expected evidence | Single-current authority chains throughout; rejections naming attempted succession targets, dual references for corrections, authorities, and reasons. |
| Pass condition | No enumerated interleaving yields two current grants, events, or corrections, and every loser is completely named and preserved. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: dual currentness, a correction advancing from the corrected record, or an incomplete rejection. Blocked: grant races cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Testing revocation-wins ordering only, missing the symmetric interleaving where the renewal lands first. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-072 — Stale-authority exercise check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a laboratory worker whose grant or lease has expired, been revoked, or been superseded cannot, by operations in exercise of it, mutate authoritative-outcome state, mutate any status dimension, append lineage-relation records, or make its output authoritative, and that output actually produced under the stale authority remains attributable to laboratory worker, grant or lease, and attempt, preserved for later disposition. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-021, WP2-SCHED-022, WP2-ISO-020, WP2-ISO-021. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; controlled-interleaving harness expiring, revoking, and superseding grants mid-operation. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Let a laboratory worker produce output, then expire, revoke, or supersede its authority and drive its late mutation and commit operations at each staleness kind. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-004 — stale or expired lease. |
| Expected evidence | Refusals of every stale mutation; the stale-produced output preserved with full attribution and pending disposition; authoritative state unchanged by the stale operations. |
| Pass condition | Stale authority mutates nothing and everything it produced survives attributably. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any stale mutation landing, or stale output lost or de-attributed. Blocked: mid-operation staleness cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Staleness enforced at operation start only, missing the exercise that straddles the expiry. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; stale output is preserved evidence, never cleaned up. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-073 — Authoritative-commit race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the authoritative-commit boundary that no interleaving of two acceptance operations for one planned run records two authoritative outcomes or one partially recorded outcome, and that the boundary is atomic or equivalently safe with the loser preserved. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-028, WP2-SCHED-030; the acceptance-uniqueness clause of WP2-ID-009. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with two eligible attempts of one planned run under a plural-attempt policy; controlled-interleaving harness at the commit boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate interleavings of two acceptance operations, including interruption between the outcome record's constituents, and a stale-commit-authority variant. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-024 — stale authority during a state transition, for the stale-commit variant; the dual-acceptance interleaving itself is forced with both operations well-formed. |
| Expected evidence | Exactly one authoritative outcome per interleaving; no partial outcome record; the losing acceptance preserved with its rejection reason. |
| Pass condition | Every interleaving yields one complete outcome and one preserved loser. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: dual outcomes, a partial record, or a lost loser. Blocked: dual eligibility cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Uniqueness enforced by a reporting-layer distinct-on-run view over a store that in fact holds two outcome records. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-074 — Cancellation-acceptance race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that a race between cancellation and authoritative acceptance for one planned run resolves under the registered, versioned authority policy the run's records identify — never by arrival order — with the resolution recorded naming the winning transition, each rejected competitor, each rejection reason, and the policy identity and version, and each defeated operation dispositioned at the recorded boundary. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-031, WP2-SCHED-032, WP2-SCHED-033, WP2-FAIL-027. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a registered authority policy; controlled-interleaving harness. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate cancellation-versus-acceptance interleavings in both presentation orders and read the resolution and disposition records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-015 — cancellation race. |
| Expected evidence | Policy-named resolution records complete in all four elements; policy-consistent outcomes identical across reversed presentation orders; dispositioned losers. |
| Pass condition | Every interleaving resolves per the named policy, order-independently, with complete resolution records. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: order-dependent outcomes, an unrecorded resolution, or an undispositioned loser. Blocked: the race cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A policy whose registered rule happens to coincide with arrival order in the enumerated cases, hiding an arrival-order implementation — reversed-order enumeration is the countermeasure and stays mandatory. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-075 — Simulation-resolution race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the resolution boundary that no interleaving of competing terminal simulation-resolution operations makes two authoritative for one logical model request or leaves one partially recorded, resolution never inferred from arrival order, and that every non-authoritative resolution operation and every late, duplicate, losing, rejected, or superseded response is preserved with a distinguishable non-authoritative disposition. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-MODEL-012, WP2-MODEL-032. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the resolution boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate races between a timeout resolution and a late-arriving acceptance path, and between two acceptance-path operations, in both orders. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-010 — late response after timeout or cancellation. |
| Expected evidence | One authoritative terminal resolution per request per interleaving; preserved non-authoritative competitors and responses; order-independent outcomes. |
| Pass condition | Every interleaving yields exactly one authoritative resolution with everything else preserved and dispositioned. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: dual resolution, partial recording, or a vanished late response. Blocked: resolution races cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | The late response arriving after the harness stops observing, so its silent promotion goes unwitnessed. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-076 — Presentation-disposition race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the presentation boundary that no interleaving of competing presentations of one request identity dispatches, charges, or resolves two of them as though no authoritative resolution stood, each further presentation receiving exactly one current typed disposition of the WP2-MODEL-055 kinds under the named policy, arrival order deciding nothing. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-MODEL-024, WP2-MODEL-055. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a registered presentation-disposition policy; scripted fake-provider fixture; controlled-interleaving harness. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate concurrent matching presentations of one unresolved request, concurrent presentations straddling its resolution, and concurrent presentations of a resolved request. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-030 — matching duplicate presentation while unresolved. |
| Expected evidence | One typed disposition per presentation; no double dispatch or double charge in any interleaving; dispositions consistent with the standing resolution state at the boundary. |
| Pass condition | Every presentation is dispositioned exactly once, and no interleaving yields a second dispatch, charge, or resolution. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: double dispatch or charge, an undispositioned presentation, or order-derived disposition. Blocked: concurrent presentations cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Deduplication keyed on transport-level message identity rather than request identity, passing while distinct transports of one request double-dispatch. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-077 — Budget-authorization race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the authorization boundary that no interleaving of concurrent authorization operations oversubscribes any applicable scope's cap or authorizes a dispatch against some applicable scopes and not others, arrival order deciding nothing, with every losing or stale authorization preserved as the fully named rejection WP2-MODEL-036 states, destroying no authorization, usage, settlement, or exposure record. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-MODEL-036 — its multi-scope race clauses; single-operation budget conduct belongs to CHK-103. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with overlapping multi-scope budgets near cap; controlled-interleaving harness. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate concurrent authorizations whose combined demand exceeds one shared scope's cap, spanning distinct scope subsets, in both orders. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-032 — budget authorization race or unresolved exposure. |
| Expected evidence | Per-interleaving: no scope oversubscribed, no split authorization, complete rejections for losers, all accounting records intact. |
| Pass condition | Caps hold and authorizations are all-scopes-or-none under every enumerated interleaving. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: oversubscription, partial-scope authorization, or a lost loser. Blocked: near-cap multi-scope states cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Scopes checked sequentially with a lock per scope, passing pairwise enumerations while a three-way interleaving splits authorization. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-078 — Credential-lifecycle race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the credential authority boundary that two operations from one current predecessor event, and two initial issuances for one exact reference and version, never both prevail; that one credential version never acquires two currentness chains; that multi-event operation membership becomes current together or not at all; that losers are preserved as the fully named non-event rejections WP2-SEC-017 states; and that no dispatch proceeds on a version a prevailing revocation has made unusable. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SEC-017, WP2-SEC-018. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic credential fixture; scripted fake-provider fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the credential boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate rotation-versus-revocation races from one predecessor, dual initial issuances, a revocation racing a dispatch that names the revoked version, and interruption inside a multi-event operation. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-033 — credential rotation/revocation race; FLT-023 — competing initial authority or credential issuance. |
| Expected evidence | Single currentness chains throughout; all-or-none multi-event membership; complete rejections; the racing dispatch either completing under a then-usable version or refused, never proceeding post-prevalence. |
| Pass condition | Every enumerated credential race resolves single-current, membership-atomic, loser-preserving, and usability-respecting. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: dual chains, a half-current operation membership, or a post-revocation dispatch. Blocked: credential races cannot be forced. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Usability read from a cache the revocation event has not invalidated, passing when the harness polls records instead of the dispatch path. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-079 — Intake-closure race check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify at the intake-closure boundary that at most one ordinary evidence-intake closure becomes current per attempt, and that a write racing the boundary is exactly one of: included in the closed snapshot, refused through a distinguishable preserved refusal naming the write and the boundary, or preserved outside the boundary as a post-intake fact — never ambiguously omitted — with every losing or superseded closure operation preserved. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-071; the ordering clause of WP2-EVID-030. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; controlled-interleaving harness at the closure boundary. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Enumerate writes landing before, astride, and after the closure acceptance, and two competing closure operations for one attempt. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-027 — evidence write racing ordinary intake closure. |
| Expected evidence | One current closure per attempt; every racing write accounted under exactly one of the three permitted outcomes; preserved losing closures. |
| Pass condition | The trichotomy is exhaustive and exclusive for every enumerated racing write, and closure currentness is single. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an ambiguously omitted write, a write both in and out of the snapshot, or dual current closures. Blocked: writes cannot be forced astride the boundary. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Snapshot membership audited against what the store now contains rather than the membership the closure record names, hiding an omitted write. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Concurrency and race testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-080 — Competing-finalizer interleaving check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify under interleaved competing finalization operations that at most one current successful finalization result exists per attempt; that every interleaved validation, derived artifact, finalization record, and candidate completion record in the still-open root remains attributable to its producing operation's never-reused identity; that the winner's validated snapshot and output membership are coherent and fully committed before its success is current, the completion record written last; and that losing, stale, interrupted, and rejected operations' outputs stay preserved and distinguishable without joining the winner's required outputs or changing its validated snapshot. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-036, WP2-EVID-072; the interleaving-facing clauses of WP2-EVID-034 and WP2-EVID-035. |
