| Purpose | Verify that every inventory identifies its exact boundary, own identity, versioned rules, and every member by stable identity and content digest with metadata sufficient to detect omission, duplication, substitution, and mutation — a full-root inventory enumerating every member of the closed boundary including the closure record and non-execution records, a subset inventory naming its narrower boundary explicitly; that membership follows boundary and contract, never convenience; that a missing member, unaccounted extra, duplicate identity, or digest conflict prevents a clean completeness verdict; that no inventory or seal claims evidence outside its own boundary; and that self-reference exclusions are explicit, never conventional. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-040, WP2-EVID-041, WP2-EVID-042, WP2-EVID-045, WP2-ISO-022. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus with complete roots and variants carrying an omission, a duplicate, a substitution, an out-of-boundary claim, and an inventory that silently omits its own seal and verification records under an unstated self-reference convention. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Build inventories over the complete and defective variants; read boundary naming, member enumeration, and each variant's verdict. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-018 — incomplete inventory. |
| Expected evidence | Complete inventories over clean roots; non-clean verdicts naming each planted defect; explicit boundary and exclusion statements throughout; the conventional-self-exclusion variant surfacing as an incomplete boundary rather than a clean verdict. |
| Pass condition | Clean roots inventory cleanly and every planted defect defeats the clean verdict. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any planted defect passing clean, an implicit self-exclusion, or a subset standing as full-root. Blocked: the variants cannot be prepared. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Membership determined by listing storage contents, which makes the inventory agree with any omission already present. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; defective variants preserved with their verdicts. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-122 — Run-level seal boundary check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that one run-level seal authenticates exactly one closed boundary in its entirety and the exact full-root inventory it names; that no seal claims a zero-member boundary where the closure record is a member, an empty execution-produced subset remaining sealable as an enumerated boundary with the empty subset explicitly stated and never a subset inventory substituting for the full root; that every seal record identifies rules and version, authority, inventory, boundary, and digest facts without requiring residence inside the boundary; that a sealed package seals exactly one identified boundary; and that per-execution sealing, root sealing, study-level sealing, and aggregation stay distinct recorded operations. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-043, WP2-EVID-044, WP2-EVID-046, WP2-ID-020, WP2-SCOPE-008. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus including an empty-execution-subset root; deterministic engine fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Seal roots including the empty-subset root; present a zero-member seal claim and a subset-for-full substitution and verify refusal; read seal records and operation distinctness. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-036 — terminal closure with an empty execution-produced subset but nonempty full root. |
| Expected evidence | Seals naming complete boundaries and inventories; the empty-subset root sealed over its enumerated nonempty boundary; refusals of the zero-member claim and the substitution; four operations distinct in records. |
| Pass condition | Every seal is boundary-exact, the empty-subset case seals honestly, and the substitutions refuse. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a zero-member claim accepted, a subset standing as the root, or merged operations. Blocked: the empty-subset root cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An empty-subset root treated as having nothing to seal, its absence of a seal then read as trivially acceptable. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-123 — Seal-verification and mutation-detection check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that seal verification is read-only, carries its own verification identity, names the exact package or seal, inventory, boundary, rules, and version verified, and preserves its verdict; that post-seal mutation, missing or extra members, digest and inventory mismatches, and package corruption each produce a typed integrity failure never repaired by silent same-identity replacement; and that a later package over a sealed boundary arrives only under a new identity with explicit correction or supersession lineage, the original preserved. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-047, WP2-EVID-048, WP2-EVID-049, WP2-EVID-050. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus; a controlled post-seal mutation applied to a copy; a supersession scenario. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Verify intact seals; mutate a sealed copy and re-verify; byte-compare roots across packaging and verification; drive a re-package with lineage. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-017 — evidence mutation after seal. |
| Expected evidence | Identified, verdict-preserving verifications; a typed integrity failure on the mutated copy; unchanged roots across operations; a lineaged successor package with the original intact. |
| Pass condition | Verification is read-only and identified, mutation is caught and typed, and repair only supersedes. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an undetected mutation, a silent re-seal, or a verifying operation that wrote. Blocked: mutation cannot be applied to a copy. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Verification reading cached digests rather than recomputing from content, blind to the mutation by construction. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; the mutated copy and its failure record preserved. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-124 — Attempt-integrity-basis composition check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that no completed, successfully finalized attempt is considered or eligible for authoritative acceptance without one exact attempt-integrity basis — naming exactly one closed own-root boundary, exactly one full-root inventory over it, exactly one run-level seal over that inventory and boundary, and exactly one verification of that chain with its preserved successful verdict, none absent — current and successful, named and verified current and successful at the authoritative-commit boundary, standing beside the WP2-SCHED-025 conditions as necessary and not sufficient, and conferring no commit authority on finalizing, sealing, or verifying authorities. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-051; the composition half of WP2-EVID-073, whose race half belongs to CHK-081. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus with complete chains and variants each missing one member — no inventory, no seal, no verification, no verdict; synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive acceptance for the complete-chain attempt and for each missing-member variant; read the acceptance's basis naming and commit-boundary verification. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-038 — a basis with a missing inventory, seal, verification, or verdict. |
| Expected evidence | Acceptance only over the complete chain, its record naming the basis; refusals for every missing-member variant naming the absent member. |
| Pass condition | The chain is genuinely mandatory member by member and acceptance verifies it at the commit boundary. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any missing-member variant accepted, or a basis assumed rather than named and verified. Blocked: chain variants cannot be prepared. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Eligibility computed from finalization success with the basis fetched decoratively afterward — probed by the no-verdict variant, which such an implementation accepts. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-125 — Post-acceptance integrity incident check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that an integrity failure detected after authoritative acceptance never rewrites the accepted outcome, remaining a preserved post-acceptance incident whose study-completion, aggregation, validity, and claim consequences flow through the Sections 14 and 15 records; and that the failing result unseats the affected basis's currentness from its recording, a further basis arriving only through a further operation over the complete recorded results. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | The post-acceptance clauses of WP2-EVID-049 and WP2-EVID-073; the consequence-propagation clause of WP2-AGG-021. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus with an accepted attempt; a controlled post-acceptance mutation of its sealed root copy in place under the same identities. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Detect the mutation through re-verification; read the accepted outcome, the basis currentness, the incident record, and the study-level consequence records. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-039 — post-acceptance integrity failure. |
| Expected evidence | The accepted outcome byte-unchanged; a preserved typed incident; the basis no longer current; consequences present as explicit records in the completion dimensions concerned. |
| Pass condition | History stands, integrity falls, and consequences propagate only through records. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a rewritten or retracted outcome, a still-current basis, or consequences applied silently. Blocked: post-acceptance mutation cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An implementation that quietly re-runs acceptance against a repaired basis, presenting continuity where supersession records are required. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-126 — Cancellation-terminal sealing check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every attempt a cancellation makes terminal — pre-execution cancellations among them — receives terminal evidence closure plus the Section 13.9 treatment over its actual closed boundary, producing a current full-root inventory and exactly one current recorded sealing outcome: a run-level seal with preserved verification verdict, or a typed, preserved sealing or integrity failure or blocker record stating why a clean seal could not be produced, the registered evidence contract never rendering inventory or outcome inapplicable and the outcome visible to the Section 15 evidence-closure-and-integrity dimension. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-FAIL-026; the cancellation-case clauses of WP2-EVID-017 and WP2-EVID-043. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture; deterministic engine fixture; a cancellation scenario whose sealing is deliberately blocked in one variant. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Cancel attempts before and during execution; drive the sealing treatment; block sealing in the variant; read inventories, outcomes, and the completion dimension's view. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-041 — cancellation-terminal sealing failure or blocker. |
| Expected evidence | Full-root inventories for every cancellation-terminal attempt; one current sealing outcome each — seal-with-verdict or typed blocker naming the blocking condition; the dimension reading them. |
| Pass condition | No cancellation-terminal attempt lacks its inventory and exactly one honest sealing outcome. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a cancellation-terminal attempt with neither seal nor blocker record, or an inapplicability escape. Blocked: the blocked-sealing variant cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Treating pre-execution cancellations as outside the sealing population, the absence of any outcome then reading as vacuous compliance. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-127 — Study-root membership and study-level sealing check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every study evidence root identifies or contains every member run root and required study-level record with explicit identity-based membership never reconstructed from layout, timestamps, or success counts; that post-closure study-level records stay outside closed attempt roots with lineage; that a study-level seal attests completeness and integrity over all registered evidence, naming the exact ledger identity and version lineage read and the current registered-membership-closure result, a complete seal recorded only against a reached closure and pre-closure seals labeled partial or snapshot, later lawful membership producing new records without rewriting; that a one-root package is never reported as a study-level seal; that the five operations stay distinct; and that later study-level packages rewrite nothing earlier. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-052, WP2-EVID-053, WP2-EVID-054, WP2-EVID-055, WP2-EVID-056, WP2-EVID-057, WP2-EVID-058, WP2-ID-019. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with several roots and one single-root study; deterministic engine fixture; a pre-closure sealing scenario. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Build study roots; seal before and after membership closure; present the one-root package as a study seal and verify refusal; read membership derivations and rewrite surfaces. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with controlled pre-closure and single-root variants; post-closure admission belongs to CHK-128. |
| Expected evidence | Identity-based memberships; snapshot-labeled pre-closure seals; a complete seal only over the reached closure naming ledger and closure result; the one-root promotion refused; earlier packages intact. |
| Pass condition | Study-level attestation is membership-exact, closure-honest, label-honest, and rewrite-free. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a discovered membership, an unlabeled snapshot seal, or a promoted single-root package. Blocked: multi-root studies cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A membership recomputed from the same storage the seal covers, incapable of noticing an unregistered omission. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-128 — Membership-closure and completion-condition check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that registered-membership closure is recorded as reached only under every WP2-AGG-016 condition — exact ledger binding, complete presence by identity, every planned run in a no-further-admission status, continuation and replacement rules permitting nothing more — with post-closure admissions refused and preserved or belonging to an expressly required new boundary; that evidence closure and integrity is reached only under every WP2-AGG-018 condition including the cancellation-sealing outcomes and current bases; that reconciliation is never claimed over represented unresolved exposure; that analysis readiness requires resolved registered rules and an identity-fixed sealed source set with named verification records; and that no dimension is reached on desired success counts. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-015, WP2-AGG-016, WP2-AGG-018, WP2-AGG-019, WP2-AGG-020. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture drivable to each reached condition and to near-miss variants — one open planned run, one missing basis, one unresolved exposure, one unresolved rule. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Drive each dimension to reached on the clean path; present reached recordings for each near-miss variant; present a post-closure admission. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-040 — post-membership-closure attempt or replacement admission. |
| Expected evidence | Reached results only on complete conditions; refusals naming the failed condition per near-miss; the post-closure admission refused and preserved or recorded under the new boundary with new lineage. |
| Pass condition | Every reached recording is condition-complete and closure genuinely closes membership. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a near-miss reaching, or a post-closure admission absorbed into the closed membership. Blocked: near-miss variants cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Closure evaluated over the ledger's planned runs while an admitted-but-unledgered unit escapes the completeness condition. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-129 — Retention and evidence-size check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every evidence-size and retention policy is versioned and names its governed classes and subjects; that capacity or size exhaustion produces a typed, evidence-preserving refusal or failure — never silent truncation, drop, overwrite, unidentified recompression, or omission; that every retention disposition is explicit, attributed, policy-versioned, and incapable of making an incomplete set appear complete or replayable; and that evidence required by an active package, claim dependency, audit, or review is never lost to an unrecorded action. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-059, WP2-EVID-060, WP2-EVID-062, WP2-EVID-063; the restart half of WP2-EVID-061 belongs to CHK-092. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with a versioned retention policy and a constrained-capacity evidence store variant. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Exhaust capacity mid-attempt; drive policy-authorized dispositions including one over package-required evidence; read refusals, disposition records, and completeness representations. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled capacity exhaustion and disposition operations; no catalogued environmental fault applies. |
| Expected evidence | Typed exhaustion refusals with everything prior preserved; attributed, versioned disposition records; the package-required disposition refused; no completeness illusion over disposed evidence. |
| Pass condition | Exhaustion and retention act only through typed, recorded, preservation-respecting paths. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any silent truncation or drop, an unrecorded disposition, or disposed evidence still presenting as complete. Blocked: capacity cannot be constrained in the venue. |
| Principal false-pass risk | An exhaustion path that buffers and retries transparently in the fixture, never reaching the refusal logic under test. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-130 — Persisted-secret discovery check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that discovery of live secret material in already-persisted or admitted evidence creates a typed, nonsecret incident record with the full WP2-SEC-014 identification, marking every affected root, inventory, seal, package, and study-level surface affected or compromised through records outside every closed or sealed boundary, none left clean and none altered in place; that the discovery blocks new clean-sealing, acceptance, and clean-aggregation claims until an explicit disposition; that it triggers the registered credential path where applicable; that handling copies the material nowhere; that quarantine or removal preserves nonsecret identity, digest, and lineage; and that sanitized derivatives are new, labeled, lineage-bound, and never claimed as originals, no seal rewritten. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-EVID-064, WP2-EVID-065, WP2-EVID-066, WP2-EVID-067, WP2-EVID-068, WP2-EVID-069, WP2-EVID-070. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus seeded post-sealing with the synthetic marker; synthetic credential fixture tying the marker to a fixture credential. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Run discovery; read incident and marking records, blocked-claim behavior, the credential-path trigger, quarantine lineage, and a produced sanitized derivative; sweep all new records for the marker. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-035 — live secret discovered after persistence or sealing. |
| Expected evidence | A complete secretless incident record; every affected surface marked from outside; sealing, acceptance, and aggregation blocked pending disposition; the rotation path invoked; a labeled, lineage-complete derivative; zero marker copies. |
| Pass condition | Discovery marks everything affected, blocks every clean claim, copies nothing, and sanitizes only into labeled derivatives. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a clean-left surface, a proceeding blocked claim, a marker copy, or an in-place alteration. Blocked: post-sealing seeding cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Marking applied to the run root alone while study-level surfaces referencing it keep presenting as clean. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; affected evidence preserved under its marking, never silently removed. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-131 — Aggregation source-set check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every aggregation identifies everything WP2-AGG-022 enumerates — study and version through the operation's own execution and concurrency provenance — with explicit absences; that source membership is established by recorded identity alone; that a primary aggregate includes at most one accepted outcome per planned run and none where none exists; that non-authoritative attempts stay referenced in audit and missingness surfaces, never promoted, erased, or counted as sample units; and that recorded deviations reach completion results, denominators, ledgers, and reports only through explicit dimension results, never silent omission, shrinkage, or adjustment. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-021, WP2-AGG-022, WP2-AGG-023, WP2-AGG-025, WP2-AGG-026. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture with accepted, failed, replaced, and excluded attempts; prepared evidence corpus of sealed sources. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce aggregations; read the full identification, membership derivation, primary-aggregate composition, and audit surfaces; permute storage in a copy and re-derive membership. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean path with a permutation probe; blocker conditions belong to CHK-132. |
| Expected evidence | Complete aggregation identifications; permutation-immune identity-based membership; per-run uniqueness in the primary aggregate; complete audit-surface references to every non-authoritative attempt. |
| Pass condition | Aggregation is identification-complete, membership-exact, and promotion-free. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a discovered membership, a promoted non-authoritative result, or an absent enumeration element. Blocked: the mixed population cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Audit surfaces populated from the primary aggregate's own inputs, structurally excluding exactly the omitted units they exist to reveal. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-132 — Aggregation-source integrity blocker check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that each WP2-AGG-024 condition — duplicate source identity, duplicate planned-run membership, dual claimed outcomes, unregistered source, omitted required member, unverifying seal, non-current or unsuccessful named basis, unverified relied-upon verification, and standing undispositioned compromise marking — prevents a clean aggregation over the source set, any permitted derived result arriving only as the labeled partial record. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-024. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus with one variant per blocker condition; synthetic registered-study fixture. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Attempt clean aggregation over each variant; read each refusal or partial-only outcome and its named blocking condition. |
| Injected fault or clean path | FLT-043 — duplicate, omitted, unverified, or compromised aggregation source; FLT-019 — missing or duplicate aggregate membership. |
| Expected evidence | No clean aggregate over any variant; per-variant records naming the blocking condition; partial records labeled where produced. |
| Pass condition | Every planted blocker blocks, named, with partial-only fallback. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any variant aggregating cleanly or blocking unnamed. Blocked: a variant cannot be prepared. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Blockers evaluated only over sources the aggregation already selected, unable to see the omitted required member. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule; variants preserved with their outcomes. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-133 — Partial-aggregate labeling check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that every partial record or aggregate is explicitly labeled partial and identifies its exact membership snapshot, source set, every unresolved unit, every missing expected source, producing analysis identity, version, and authority, and each respect in which it is not final or claim-eligible; that it never becomes final by renaming, overwriting, relocation, or in-place addition; that later aggregates are new identities with explicit source and supersession lineage, every earlier aggregate preserved; that partials are never reported as final or claim-eligible; and that producing one resolves no other completion dimension. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-006, WP2-AGG-027, WP2-AGG-028, WP2-AGG-029, WP2-AGG-030. |
| Fixtures and setup | Synthetic registered-study fixture mid-execution; prepared evidence corpus. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Produce partials; present rename, relocate, and append promotions and verify refusal; produce the final aggregate and read supersession lineage and dimension states. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — controlled promotion operations. |
| Expected evidence | Fully labeled partials; refused promotions; a new-identity final aggregate with lineage; earlier partials preserved; unchanged sibling dimensions. |
| Pass condition | Partiality is explicit, promotion-proof, supersession-clean, and dimension-inert. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: an unlabeled partial, a successful promotion, or a superseded aggregate lost. Blocked: mid-execution state cannot be staged. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A partial flag stored outside the aggregate artifact, lost exactly under the relocation the promotion exploits. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

**CHK-134 — Deterministic-rebuild and derived-output check.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify that aggregations and partials are written outside the immutable boundaries they read and never replace, mutate, reorganize, conceal, or write into source evidence; that a registered deterministic aggregation reproduces the same derived output from exact sealed sources, analysis identity and version, rules and version, and identified environment, or produces a typed rebuild failure, never a silently different result; that stochastic or model-assisted aggregation states that fact with the charter-required model, prompt, uncertainty, and stochastic lineage and non-engine-native status, its output never treated as deterministic or authoritative world truth; and that changed rules, sources, or corrections produce new identities or versions with every existing aggregate preserved. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-AGG-031, WP2-AGG-032, WP2-AGG-033, WP2-AGG-034, WP2-AGG-035. |
| Fixtures and setup | Prepared evidence corpus of sealed sources; a registered deterministic aggregation and a registered model-assisted variant with synthetic lineage; a perturbed-environment rebuild variant. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Aggregate; byte-compare sources before and after; rebuild identically and under the perturbed environment; run the model-assisted variant; change a rule and re-aggregate. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — clean rebuilds with a controlled environment perturbation. |
| Expected evidence | Untouched sources; identical rebuilds or a typed rebuild failure under perturbation; a fully lineaged model-assisted record; a new identity for the changed-rule aggregate with predecessors preserved. |
| Pass condition | Aggregation is source-inert, rebuild-honest, assistance-explicit, and version-disciplined. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: a source write, a silently different rebuild, or an unlabeled model-assisted output. Blocked: rebuild cannot run twice in the venue. |
| Principal false-pass risk | A rebuild reading cached intermediate results, identical for the wrong reason — countered by the perturbation variant that caches would survive but honest rebuilds fail typed. |
| Cleanup or preservation | General preservation rule. |
| Venue | Evidence-integrity testing; local or CI. |

#### 2.2.7 Later registered-study-evidence checks (CHK-135 through CHK-137)

The checks of this class read evidence from later, separately authorized registered studies once such evidence lawfully exists. They are supplementary by construction: none of them is, or can become, an implementation-acceptance pass condition; each supplements a named keyless check and never substitutes for it; and a blocked or not-run outcome here — the normal state until such a study exists — affects no acceptance verdict.

**CHK-135 — Registered-study concurrency-provenance supplement.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Supplement CHK-111 by verifying, over a later registered study's evidence, that observed concurrency-provenance records were produced with the required observer, time-domain, and profile identification under genuine operational contention, and that material registered-versus-observed divergences were recorded, attributed, and retained. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCHED-039, WP2-SCHED-040, WP2-SCHED-042 — as supplementary evidence only; their acceptance basis remains CHK-111 and CHK-109. |
| Fixtures and setup | None — the sealed evidence of a later, separately authorized registered study, read in place. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Read-only audit of provenance and divergence records within the study's sealed evidence. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — read-only audit of evidence produced elsewhere; no fault is ever injected into study evidence. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record over the study's provenance and divergence records, citing the sealed sources read. |
| Pass condition | The audited records carry the required identifications and divergence treatment under operational conditions. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: audited records missing required identification or divergence treatment — feeding the open-questions and incident paths, never retroactively failing an acceptance. Blocked: no such study evidence exists — the normal state. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Treating this supplement's pass as strengthening the acceptance verdict, which rests on the keyless checks alone. |
| Cleanup or preservation | Read-only; the study evidence is never modified, and the audit record joins the conformance-evidence package. |
| Venue | Later registered-study evidence; later registered-study evidence venue, supplementary only. |

**CHK-136 — Live-provider behavior supplement.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Supplement CHK-108 by verifying, over a later registered study's evidence, that provider-behavior observations for phenomena that in fact occurred on live request paths — rate limits, outages, substitutions, late completions among them — carry the full required identification with explicit absences. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-MODEL-049, WP2-MODEL-050 — as supplementary evidence only; their acceptance basis remains CHK-108. |
| Fixtures and setup | None — the sealed evidence of a later, separately authorized registered study, read in place. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Read-only audit of provider-behavior observation records against the identification contract. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — read-only audit; live provider behavior is observed, never induced, by conformance activity. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record over the observation records, citing sealed sources. |
| Pass condition | Every audited observation is identification-complete for what in fact occurred and was observable. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: incomplete identifications in audited records — routed as incidents and open questions, never as retroactive acceptance failure. Blocked: no such study evidence exists — the normal state. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Reading the scarcity of observed phenomena in a quiet study as evidence the observation contract works. |
| Cleanup or preservation | Read-only; audit record into the conformance-evidence package. |
| Venue | Later registered-study evidence; later registered-study evidence venue, supplementary only. |

**CHK-137 — Parity-claim boundary supplement.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Purpose | Verify, over the reporting of any later registered study, that no conformance result, concurrency profile, provenance record, comparison, throughput result, or sealed or reproducible aggregation is reported or cited as evidence of serial-versus-parallel behavioral parity, neutrality, or interchangeability, and that nothing is treated as beginning, satisfying, or substituting for the separately authorized Work Package 3 study. |
| Mapped requirement IDs | WP2-SCOPE-011, WP2-SCHED-050, WP2-AGG-040, WP2-AGG-041, WP2-HAND-027, WP2-HAND-031, WP2-HAND-037, WP2-HAND-038 — the operational-reporting half of the rows that cite this check, each of which also cites a static implementation-acceptance check of its documentation half, CHK-007 or CHK-020. |
| Fixtures and setup | None — the published reporting of a later, separately authorized registered study. |
| Controlled operation or observation | Read-only audit of every parity-adjacent claim in the study's reporting. |
| Injected fault or clean path | None — read-only audit. |
| Expected evidence | An audit record enumerating parity-adjacent claims and their claim-boundary conformance. |
| Pass condition | No audited surface carries a prohibited parity inference or Work Package 3 substitution. |
| Fail and blocked conditions | Fail: any prohibited claim — routed to the claim-boundary incident path. Blocked: no such reporting exists — the normal state. |
| Principal false-pass risk | Auditing formal conclusions while promotional or summary materials carry the parity inference. |
| Cleanup or preservation | Read-only; audit record into the conformance-evidence package. |
| Venue | Later registered-study evidence; later registered-study evidence venue, supplementary only. |

### 2.3 The requirement-to-check matrix

One subsection follows per requirement namespace, in specification document order. Each subsection carries one table with exactly one row per requirement identifier of its namespace, under the row contract Section 2.1 states.

#### 2.3.1 WP2-SCOPE requirements

| Requirement ID | Behavior or invariant tested | Conceptual check ID(s) | Keyless conformance-test class | Required fixture(s) | Required fault injection or explicit none | Expected observable evidence | Conceptual pass condition | Principal false-pass risk | Execution venue | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WP2-SCOPE-001 | Concurrent independent runs each retain their own identity, configuration, mutable and stochastic state, budgets, timing records, replay lineage, and per-attempt failure histories and evidence roots | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | Per-run identity, configuration, state, stochastic-state, budget, timing, and replay-lineage records; per-attempt failure histories and evidence roots; overlap-demonstrating schedule records | Under recorded genuinely overlapping execution, every retained property of every run resolves from its own records to exactly one owning run, each attempt's failure history and evidence root are distinct and complete, and the injected contamination is detected and refused | Runs scheduled with no real overlap, so contamination had no opportunity to occur | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCOPE-002 | Concurrent studies on shared infrastructure each retain their own identity, configuration, mutable state, budget scope, treatment, storage namespace, and evidence lineage | CHK-084 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-03 (two-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-022 | Per-study identity, configuration, state, budget-scope, treatment, namespace, and lineage records together with shared-infrastructure placement records | With recorded overlap on shared infrastructure, each study's seven retained properties resolve to that study alone and the injected cross-study contamination is detected and refused | The two studies never actually share the contended infrastructure component | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCOPE-003 | Sharing infrastructure never merges any retained property of two studies or of two independent runs | CHK-084, CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-03 (two-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-022, FLT-021 | Cross-unit record comparisons over the co-resident studies and runs and the contamination refusal records | Record comparison across the co-resident studies and runs shows every named property held as a distinct per-unit record, with each injected contamination surfaced as a refusal rather than a merged record | Merging checked only on identity fields while state or lineage records are silently shared | CI | Implementation acceptance |

| WP2-ID-001 | Every register-entity instance carries an explicitly recorded identity not derived solely from storage path, wall-clock timestamp, ordering, or an under-scoped local identifier | CHK-023, CHK-008 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-03 (two-study) | FLT-001 | Identity records for every created instance showing their recorded identity basis, plus the typed duplicate-identity refusal record | Every probed instance resolves through an explicitly recorded identity that survives permutation of paths, timestamps, and record order, and the injected duplicate run identity is refused with a typed refusal record | Probe samples only instances whose identities happen to be explicitly recorded while path-derived identities of other entity kinds go unsampled | CI | Implementation acceptance |

| WP2-ID-003 | Assigned identities distinguish their instances throughout the register-stated scope, including across concurrently running studies and attempts | CHK-023, CHK-008 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-03 (two-study) | FLT-001 | Identity records created concurrently in two distinct studies, plus the duplicate refusal record | Instances created concurrently in distinct studies carry distinct resolvable identities, and the injected duplicate run identity is refused rather than coexisting under one identity | Uniqueness verified only within a single study so a cross-study collision never enters the probe | CI | Implementation acceptance |

#### 2.3.3 WP2-ISO requirements

| Requirement ID | Behavior or invariant tested | Conceptual check ID(s) | Keyless conformance-test class | Required fixture(s) | Required fault injection or explicit none | Expected observable evidence | Conceptual pass condition | Principal false-pass risk | Execution venue | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP2-ISO-001 | Infrastructure sharing never merges unit identities, registered treatments, mutable state, evidence, or budget attribution at any isolation level | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | Per-unit identity, treatment, mutable-state, evidence, and budget records produced under genuine concurrency on shared infrastructure, plus the injected contamination's disposition record | Every governed record of every kind resolves to exactly one unit, no two units' record sets intersect, and the injected contamination is refused or detected and attributed rather than absorbed as a merge | Units run serially or on disjoint infrastructure, so the merge path is never under pressure and clean separation is trivial | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-002 | Every governed mutable record held in a shared service partitions by study, planned-run, and attempt lineage to exactly one owning unit | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | The shared service's record set with each record's lineage keys and owner resolution | Each governed record in the shared service resolves through its recorded lineage to exactly one owning unit, records from at least two units present, and the contamination probe leaves no doubly owned or ownerless record | Only one unit's records occupy the service at probe time, so the partition is never contested | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-003 | No independent run or attempt reads, overwrites, finalizes, cancels, cleans up, or adopts another's mutable state | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | Per-unit mutable-state operation histories with actor attribution and the cross-unit attempt's disposition | Each unit's mutable state shows only operations carried out under its own lineage, and the injected cross-unit operation is refused or provably without effect, its disposition preserved | The probe never actually issues a cross-unit operation, so absence of contamination reflects absence of stimulus | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-004 | Cross-unit evidence operations are prohibited while shared-asset reads and pre-action verification reads remain permitted | CHK-083, CHK-030, CHK-028 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness), FIX-09 (prepared evidence corpus) | FLT-021, FLT-002 | Per-unit evidence access and operation records typed by purpose; shared-asset read records; collision-verification records | No unit's evidence carries a write, finalization, seal, cancellation, cleanup, or adoption by another unit, while an actually exercised shared-asset read and an actually exercised pre-action verification read both complete and are recorded as permitted | Access records lack purpose typing, letting an adoption dressed as a verification read pass as permitted | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-005 | Two execution attempts of one planned run never continue one another in place | CHK-083, CHK-088 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness), FIX-08 (crash-and-restart harness) | FLT-021, FLT-006 | Both attempts' identities, evidence roots, transition histories, and lineage records | The second attempt carries its own identity and root, the interrupted attempt keeps a terminal disposition with its records unchanged, and no record shows either attempt resuming or extending the other's execution state | The second attempt never touches state the first produced, so in-place continuation was never possible to attempt | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-006 | Mutable in-memory state is never shared between or carried over across isolation units | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | Recorded state, configuration, and stochastic provenance derivations for each unit | Every unit's recorded state derivations trace exclusively to its own registered inputs, and the injected in-memory carry-over is refused or surfaces as a detected contamination rather than silently shaping a successor unit | Only persisted state is inspected, so in-memory leakage that leaves no durable trace escapes the probe | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-007 | Every unit writes mutable state and evidence only into a namespace bound to its own recorded identity and disjoint from same-level peers | CHK-083 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-021 | Namespace binding records and the recorded location of every write | Every observed write lies inside the namespace bound to the writing unit's recorded identity, same-level namespaces are pairwise disjoint through that binding, and the injected cross-namespace write is refused | Disjointness judged from path naming conventions instead of the recorded identity binding | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-008 | Shared treatment-contributing assets are content-addressed or version-pinned, read-only for the whole use, and recorded per sharing unit by exact identity or digest | CHK-030 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine) | None | Each sharing unit's evidence entry naming the asset's exact identity or digest, plus the asset's pinning and read-only facts | An asset actually shared by at least two units appears in every sharing unit's evidence by exact identity or content digest with its version pinning recorded, its content identical for all consumers for the whole duration of use | The fixture shares no treatment-contributing asset, so the clause is never exercised | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-009 | Every treatment-contributing configuration value resolves from the unit's own registered configuration and is recorded with its evidence | CHK-030 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine) | None | Per-unit configuration-resolution records and evidence entries | Every treatment-contributing value in effect traces to the unit's own registered configuration and appears with its evidence, demonstrated while ambient values deliberately disagree with registered ones | Ambient and registered values coincide, making mis-resolution unobservable | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-010 | Ambient configuration or environment values determine treatment-bearing properties only where recorded against the unit for which they took effect | CHK-030 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine) | None | Treatment-property derivations and per-unit ambient-value records | Under controlled ambient variation, no treatment-bearing property differs between units without a per-unit record of the ambient value that took effect | The varied ambient values touch no treatment-bearing property, so no violation could arise | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-011 | Mutable random-generator and stochastic-event state is never shared between, inherited by, or carried over into isolation units | CHK-031 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine) | None | Per-unit stochastic-state provenance, seed and derivation records, and realized event streams | Each unit's realized stochastic events derive only from its own registered inputs and reproduce from them alone, no derivation reading another unit's generator state | Streams that differ for unrelated reasons mask a shared generator the probe never directly interrogates | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-ISO-012 | A registered pairing design fixes identity scheme, derivation rule, master inputs, and branch-conditional rule before execution, with every realized paired input identified per consuming unit with event-level lineage | CHK-031 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-04 (deterministic engine) | None | The registered design record with its pre-execution registration facts; each consuming unit's paired-input identifications and lineage | Every design element is recorded before any execution, and every realized paired input is identified in every consuming unit's evidence with lineage from which cross-unit correspondence, or a counterpart's absence, is read from records alone | Correspondence reconstructed afterwards from equal values rather than read from recorded lineage | CI | Implementation acceptance |

| WP2-LIFE-032 | Replacement and supersession relation records conform to every clause of the Section 8.6 lineage-relation collection block | CHK-009, CHK-025, CHK-034, CHK-040 | static document audit | FIX-01 (record-audit corpus), FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study) | FLT-020 | The documented lineage-relation record model, plus relation records produced by replacement operations read field by field | The documented model states every block clause, and each produced relation record carries record type, identity, participants, planned run, policy identity and version, authority, reason, time-domain facts, named evidence, and correction rule, with no observed effect on any scalar dimension, produced evidence, or authoritative outcome | Documentation audit passes on paraphrase while produced records omit block fields the dynamic checks never exercise | static; CHK-025, CHK-034, and CHK-040 local or CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-LIFE-033 | Operational and finalization dimensions coordinated at one atomic coherence decision into all-or-none compound outcomes with every unaccepted competitor preserved | CHK-070, CHK-089 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness), FIX-08 (crash-and-restart harness) | FLT-007, FLT-026 | Paired Finalizing and Finalization-in-progress transition records, compound-outcome records, and preserved competitor rejection records under each enumerated interleaving and the finalization-crash variant | Under every enumerated interleaving and the crash variant, exactly one coherent compound pair is current — Completed with its named Finalization succeeded result, or Failed or Cancelled with the generic Finalization failed result — the entry pair sharing one triggering identity, and every unaccepted competitor preserved supplying a current value in neither dimension | Interleavings arise from timing luck rather than forced schedules, so the partial-outcome window is never actually entered | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-LIFE-034 | At most one transition per current revision, one initial recording per dimension, and one correction per corrected record, decided at an atomic boundary without arrival-order inference | CHK-069 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-024 | Currentness records after each forced competing pair, in original and mirrored presentation orders | Under every enumerated competing pair exactly one operation is current afterwards, and mirrored-order reruns of the same pair produce the same currentness structure independent of arrival order | The race window is never actually forced, so single-writer behavior passes as boundary safety | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-LIFE-035 | Losing or stale transition, initial, or correction operations preserved as rejections naming entity, dimension, claimed predecessor or character, attempted value, authority, and reason, destroying nothing | CHK-069 | concurrency and race testing | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study), FIX-07 (controlled-interleaving harness) | FLT-024 | Rejection records for every forced loser plus pre-race and post-race digests of status, history, and evidence | Every forced losing or stale operation yields a rejection record carrying all six named elements, and pre-race digests of recorded status, transition history, and evidence match post-race read-back | The loser is silently dropped while the checker confirms only that the winner's record is well formed | CI | Implementation acceptance |

#### 2.3.5 WP2-SCHED requirements

| Requirement ID | Behavior or invariant tested | Conceptual check ID(s) | Keyless conformance-test class | Required fixture(s) | Required fault injection or explicit none | Expected observable evidence | Conceptual pass condition | Principal false-pass risk | Execution venue | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP2-SCHED-001 | Each of the eight scheduling and authority event kinds is recorded as a distinct, separately identified, separately attributable event | CHK-047 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Event records for queue presence, delivery, assignment, grant, admission, release, start, and acceptance, each with its own identity and Section 6 entity attribution | Every event kind the fixture drives yields its own event record with a distinct identity and resolvable entity attribution, no two kinds sharing one record | A single combined record carrying several kind labels counted as eight distinct events | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-002 | Queue presence, delivery, or dequeuing never stands alone as execution or transition authority | CHK-047 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Delivery and dequeue records for a unit withheld from grant, with no execution start or transition naming them as authority | A delivered-but-ungranted unit shows no execution activity and no transition whose recorded authority is the delivery, dequeue, or queue presence itself; execution follows only a recorded grant | No delivered-but-ungranted window is ever exercised, leaving the prohibition satisfied vacuously | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-003 | Every admitted attempt names exactly one owning laboratory-worker identity from admission through and beyond terminality | CHK-048 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Attempt records with the owner relation inspected at admission, mid-lifecycle, and after a terminal value | Each admitted attempt shows one and the same owner identity at every inspection point, including after terminality | Owner inspected only at admission, so a later reassignment goes unobserved | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-004 | An execution-attempt identity is never reassigned to a laboratory worker other than its admission owner | CHK-048 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Refusal or no-effect record for a controlled reassignment operation; unchanged owner relation | A controlled reassignment operation leaves the recorded owner unchanged and is itself recorded as refused or without effect | No reassignment operation is actually attempted, so the prohibition passes vacuously | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-005 | Repeated delivery of one assignment resolves idempotently, creating no further attempt identity, no further root, and no second grant | CHK-049 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | FLT-009 | Post-duplicate-delivery record set: attempt identities, run evidence roots, grants, and idempotent-resolution records | After the injected duplicate deliveries verifiably reached intake, exactly one attempt identity, one root, and one grant exist, each repeat carrying a recorded idempotent resolution | The duplicate delivery is silently dropped before intake, so the counts were never at risk | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-006 | A conflicting delivery, assignment, or wave-membership record is refused with a distinguishable refusal naming the conflicting facts, never merged, reconciled, or partially applied | CHK-049 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | FLT-009 | Refusal record naming both the recorded and the conflicting facts; unchanged attempt record | The injected conflicting record produces a refusal record naming both fact sets while the attempt's recorded assignment and wave membership remain unchanged in full | The conflict is silently discarded with no refusal record and the absence of change is read as compliance | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-007 | An additional physical execution is admitted as a new attempt with its own identity, root, owning laboratory-worker identity, and grant | CHK-039 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture), FIX-04 (deterministic engine fixture) | None | Second attempt's admission records: fresh attempt identity, distinct run evidence root, own owner relation, own grant, and the admission decision record | The further execution appears as a newly admitted attempt whose identity, root, owner, and grant all differ from the first attempt's, with the admission decision recorded | Only one execution is ever driven, so the new-attempt rule is never exercised | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-008 | A further physical execution is never recorded or reported as a transport retry, restart, resume, or continuation | CHK-039, CHK-044 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture), FIX-04 (deterministic engine fixture), FIX-05 (scripted fake-provider fixture), FIX-08 (crash-and-restart harness) | None | Classification and reporting records for the further execution across the seven-operation vocabulary | Every record and reporting surface classifies the further execution as a separately admitted attempt and none of the four prohibited kinds appears anywhere for it | A reporting surface outside the inspected set carries the prohibited classification | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-009 | Plurality of nonterminal attempts per planned run is fixed by the registered, versioned scheduling policy the run's records identify | CHK-050, CHK-018 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-01 (record-audit corpus), FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Run records naming scheduling-policy identity and version; admission outcomes under a single-attempt and a plural-permitting policy | A second nonterminal admission is refused under the single-attempt policy and admitted under the plural-permitting policy, each run's records identifying the governing policy identity and version | Only one policy variant is exercised, so policy control is inferred rather than observed | CI | Implementation acceptance |
| WP2-SCHED-010 | The simultaneous nonterminal attempt count is never determined by an unrecorded infrastructure, delivery, or recovery property | CHK-050 | deterministic keyless execution | FIX-02 (synthetic registered-study fixture) | None | Admission outcomes under varied delivery and recovery behavior with the registered policy held fixed | Varying delivery and recovery behavior never changes the permitted count, which follows only the recorded policy identification | Infrastructure behavior is never varied, so an unrecorded determinant stays invisible | CI | Implementation acceptance |
