| Deliberately unselected | No ledger store, accounting schema, or numeric operating parameter; the near-cap states are contract positions — capacity states relative to registered caps — and no cap value carries any significance beyond forcing the governed paths. |

### 4.3 The suite cases

The suite holds fifteen cases, CASE-01 through CASE-15, spanning the ten coverage areas of this conformance contract in order: static specification and matrix audit; clean isolated single-attempt execution; parallel independent-run isolation; parallel independent-study isolation; scheduling, lease, grant, cancellation, and authoritative-commit races; request idempotency, retry, fallback, budget, timing, and provider-behavior accounting; restart, recovery, and replacement paths; replay, intake closure, finalization, inventory, sealing, verification, and integrity-basis paths; study membership, missingness, partial aggregation, clean aggregation, and zero-evidence-study paths; and secret-boundary and sanitized-derivative paths using only synthetic, non-live fixtures. Each area is covered by one or two cases, and each case names its area.

Two coverage properties hold across the cases and are mechanically checkable. First, every keyless conceptual check of the Section 2.2 catalogue — CHK-001 through CHK-134 — is a constituent of at least one case, and the three supplementary checks CHK-135 through CHK-137 are constituents of none. Second, every fault of the Section 3 catalogue — FLT-001 through FLT-044 — is injected by at least one case, so the required negative and fault detections of the whole catalogue are exercised within the suite rather than left to unassembled checks.

**CASE-01 — Static specification, matrix, and reporting-surface audit.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Coverage area | Static specification and matrix audit. |
| Constituent conceptual checks | CHK-001, CHK-002, CHK-003, CHK-004, CHK-005, CHK-006, CHK-007, CHK-008, CHK-009, CHK-010, CHK-011, CHK-012, CHK-013, CHK-014, CHK-015, CHK-016, CHK-017, CHK-018, CHK-019, CHK-020, CHK-021. |
| Fixtures | FIX-01; FIX-02 for the policy inventory CHK-018 audits; FIX-06 for the credential-reference forms CHK-017 audits. |
| Controlled interleavings or injected faults | None — clean-path document audit throughout; nothing executes in this case. |
| Expected evidence package | The audit records of every constituent check, each citing every artifact read: the three bound identities per conformance artifact; the two extracted requirement-ID sets with both set differences and the per-row check-resolution report; the outcome-vocabulary, keyless-boundary, terminology, selection-prohibition, and claim-boundary audit records; the clause-by-clause conformance tables for the eight audited record models and registers; the policy-by-policy identification table; and, where the artifacts exist, the plan resolution table of CHK-019, the gate classification of CHK-020, and the register audit of CHK-021. |
| Conceptual pass condition | Every constituent audit passes on its catalogue terms — both set differences empty, every audited clause represented, no prohibited claim or selection on any audited surface, and no collapsed outcome vocabulary. Where any constituent's subject artifacts do not yet exist, CHK-019 before an authorized implementation plan among them, that constituent is recorded blocked or not run and the case is blocked rather than passed, no blocked constituent ever being folded into a case pass. |

## 5. Evidence required from conformance runs

No conformance run is authorized at this head, and this section performs, schedules, or implements none. It defines the evidence contract of a future authorized conformance run: what the run's evidence package identifies for its results to be evaluable under the principles of Section 1 and creditable under the acceptance criteria of Section 8. The contract states conceptual content only. No evidence format, serialization, store, archive technology, digest algorithm, or package layout is selected here — WP2-SCOPE-015 prohibits the specification package from selecting one — and the realization of the conformance-evidence package is a future implementation-plan matter inside the handoff boundary Section 18 of the main specification states.

The evidence package of one conformance run is complete only where it identifies all of the following, for the run as a whole and for every check result within it.

| Evidence element | What the package identifies |
| --- | --- |
| Specification and conformance-document versions | The exact established main-specification version and the exact conformance-document version the run assessed against — the first two members of the exact-version triple of Section 1. |
| Implementation build and source identity | The exact implementation build and version assessed — the third member of the triple — and the exact source identity from which that build was produced. |
| Check, fault, fixture, suite, and environment identities | The identity, and version where versioned, of every conceptual check realized, every fault injected, every fixture used, every suite case executed, and every execution environment involved, so that each realized test artifact is joined explicitly to the `CHK-###`, `FLT-###`, `FIX-##`, or `CASE-##` entry it realizes. |
| Concurrency parameters and observed provenance | The registered or controlled concurrency parameters under which every concurrency-bearing check ran, and the actually observed execution provenance, kept separate and explicitly joined, mirroring the registered-versus-observed separation Section 10 of the main specification states. |
| Operation and authority histories | The operation and authority histories necessary to evaluate each check — admissions, grants, leases, transitions, commits, cancellations, competing and losing operations among them — together with the authority under which each operation occurred. |
| Domain records | All request, timing, budget, evidence-root, replay, finalization, seal, verification, and aggregation records relevant to the checks exercised. |
| Result identity, outcome, reason, and sources | For every check result: its own stable result identity, its outcome in the Section 1 vocabulary, the reason for that outcome, and explicit references to the source evidence supporting it. |
| Preserved failures, blocked checks, losing operations, and cleanup | Every failed and blocked check with its complete evidence, every losing or superseded operation a race or fault check produced, every injected fault's residue, and the outcome of every cleanup — completed, failed, or not performed — preserved rather than discarded, in the Section 1 preservation discipline. |
| Requirement-coverage report | A complete requirement-coverage report over the exact requirement-ID set of the bound specification version: for every requirement, its mapped checks, their outcomes, and whether a current passing basis exists, with no requirement omitted. |
| Sealed nonsecret package | The whole of the above as one nonsecret, sealed, verifiable conformance-evidence package: its contents enumerated by an inventory, the inventory and its boundary authenticated by a seal, and the seal verifiable read-only, mirroring the layering Section 13 of the main specification states for laboratory evidence, with no live secret on any surface of the package. |

Every fixture the package references is synthetic and non-live. A fixture standing in for secret material is described and marked as synthetic rather than realized as a value resembling a live secret, and the package carries no live provider credential, session, or token in any form, consistent with the boundary Section 16 of the main specification states.

Where later registered-study evidence supplements an implementation-acceptance check, the package identifies that evidence as later registered-study evidence, joined by identity to the registration it came from and kept distinct from the keyless basis it supplements. It never substitutes for the keyless basis, and its absence never blocks an implementation-acceptance outcome the keyless basis supports.

Conformance evidence is not study evidence for a claim about a candidate cognitive architecture, and it is not Work Package 3 parity evidence, merely because it is sealed, complete, deterministic, or reproducible. The evidence package of a conformance run supports exactly one kind of claim — that the assessed implementation build satisfied, or did not satisfy, identified requirements of the exact bound specification version under identified checks — and Section 6 states the boundary that keeps every further claim out of reach.

## 6. Boundary between implementation conformance and Work Package 3 parity

Passing the keyless implementation-acceptance suite of Section 4, and implementation acceptance under Section 8, may make an implementation eligible for a separately authorized, separately registered, preregistered serial-versus-parallel parity study under Work Package 3, as Section 19 of the main specification states. Eligibility is the entire effect: the parity study itself remains unauthorized until its own separate authorization exists, and no conformance outcome creates, advances, or implies that authorization.

Implementation acceptance establishes none of the following, and no conformance result is reported as establishing any of them: serial-versus-parallel behavioral equivalence, neutrality, or interchangeability, which WP2-SCOPE-011 makes unreportable from a conformance result; behavioral quality, believability, semantic correctness, observer value, the scientific validity of any study, or the success of any candidate cognitive architecture, which WP2-SCOPE-012 makes unreportable from a conformance result; and authorization to begin Work Package 3 — Work Package 3 remains separately registered and separately authorized, as Section 19 of the main specification states, and WP2-SCOPE-014 already withholds any such authorization from approval, completion, or merge of the specification package itself.

No aggregate, deterministic rebuild, seal, throughput result, or completion record substitutes for behavioral parity evidence. WP2-AGG-040 keeps a sealed, rebuilt, or deterministically reproducible study aggregation from being reported or cited as evidence of parity, neutrality, or interchangeability, and WP2-AGG-041 keeps completion of any study, production of any aggregate, and conformance with the completion and aggregation contract of Section 15 from being treated as beginning, satisfying, or substituting for the Work Package 3 parity study, WP2-HAND-026 and WP2-HAND-038 carrying the same denial for conformance and implementation acceptance generally. Conformance evidence is likewise not parity evidence merely because it is sealed, complete, deterministic, or reproducible, as Section 5 states.

Until parity is earned within a preregistered claim boundary, concurrency remains a recorded experimental factor, as Section 19 of the main specification states, and no conformance result changes that. Section 19 also records that an implementation or concurrency-profile change may require renewed conformance and may unseat the applicability of earlier parity evidence: a parity result, once earned, attaches to what it was earned on, and a conformance verdict for a later build neither carries an earlier parity result forward nor re-establishes it.

The three gates Section 20 of the main specification keeps distinct stay distinct here: Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance, future implementation acceptance, and Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence. Parity is never inferred from either earlier gate, this document's Section 8 states criteria for the second gate only, and the matrix rows of Section 2 whose Boundary carries the parity-claim-boundary annotation mark exactly the requirements whose subject is parity non-inference, without moving any of them into an implementation-acceptance pass condition.

## 7. Unresolved conformance questions

This register records genuine unresolved conformance questions and nothing else. An entry never weakens a requirement of the main specification, never defers an obligation assigned to Chunk 7, and never conceals an untestable ambiguity, and a material question that would leave any requirement without a conceptual pass condition cannot be recorded here at all, because such a question blocks approval of this document rather than standing as an open item; no such question exists at this head. The register is append-only: entries are identified `CQ-##` sequentially in order of recording, a later ruling arrives as a further identified entry with explicit reference to the entry it resolves or supersedes, no entry is rewritten, and the Section 21 register of the main specification remains where the underlying open question and any later explicit ruling are recorded for the specification itself — this register records the conformance consequence.

Each entry states the same ten fields: the question; its source in the approved specification record; the exact affected requirements and checks; whether it blocks implementation selection, implementation acceptance, or neither, with the basis for that status; what it does affect; decision authority and latest decision point; evidence needed to decide; behavioral versus operational consequence; versioning consequence; and current status.

One entry exists.

**CQ-01. Independent format verification of a future packaging version.**

| Field | Statement |
| --- | --- |
| Question | Whether and how a future evidence-packaging version is required to be independently format-verified — verified as to the container or archive format of the package it produces, by an independent observation such as a clean extraction, beyond the digest attestation its receipt carries. |
| Source | Section 5.14 item 4 of the main specification records this as a later question that Section 5 does not answer. Specification Section 21.3 records the specification-side entry as open question `OQ-01`, of which this entry records the conformance consequence. Section 5.9 records the confirmed Milestone 2 archive-format defect that raised it, in which a receipt attested a digest over the committed archive without detecting that its container format diverged from the stated packaging contract. The Section 13.9 rationale expressly carries the question forward, deliberately not settling it. |
| Affected requirements and checks | WP2-EVID-044, WP2-EVID-046, WP2-EVID-047, and WP2-EVID-048, in the narrow sense that the versioned sealing, packaging, and verification rules those requirements name are where an affirmative resolution would take effect, together with the conceptual checks the Section 2 matrix maps to those requirements. The conceptual pass conditions of those checks are complete and stand unaffected under either answer: on this question, WP2-EVID-048 requires of a verification only that it be performed under identified rules and version with its verdict preserved, as the Section 13.9 rationale states, and the attempt-integrity-basis chain of WP2-EVID-051 and WP2-EVID-073 stands unaffected. |
| Blocking status | Blocks neither implementation selection nor implementation acceptance. Not implementation selection, because the archive- and serialization-technology decision of the Section 18 register of the main specification can be taken under either answer — the properties any selection has to satisfy are stated by WP2-EVID-040 through WP2-EVID-050 and stand unaffected. Not implementation acceptance, because no requirement loses its conceptual pass condition: every check over the sealing, packaging, and verification requirements passes or fails on the same stated conditions under either answer. |
| What it does affect | The content of the versioned verification rules a registered evidence contract adopts — whether those rules include independent container-format verification — and thereby the strength of the format half of sealed-package verification in future evidence; the evidence-needed field of the corresponding Section 18 handoff decision; and, under an affirmative ruling, the observations recorded by the checks mapped to WP2-EVID-044 and WP2-EVID-048, reached only as a versioned revision under the versioning consequence below. |
| Decision authority and latest decision point | The Operator with applicable Advisor review, through an explicit ruling recorded in the Section 21.3 register of the main specification as a further entry carrying lineage to `OQ-01`. Latest decision point: before a future implementation plan or study registration fixes the versioned packaging and verification rules a registered evidence contract adopts, and in every case before any conformance run whose checks observe those rules. |
| Evidence needed to decide | The Milestone 2 closeout record of the archive-format defect that Section 5.9 cites, already in repository evidence; the properties of the archive or serialization family a future implementation plan proposes under the Section 18 register; and an assessment of what independent format verification adds over digest attestation for that family, the Section 5.9 record establishing that a digest receipt alone did not detect container-format divergence. |
| Behavioral versus operational consequence | Operational only. Format verification governs how already-produced evidence is packaged and verified after execution; it reaches no treatment, no execution-time behavior, and no candidate cognitive architecture, and under either answer the evidence bytes and their digest attestation are unchanged. |
| Versioning consequence | An affirmative resolution arrives as a new versioned evidence-packaging and verification-rules identity, the historical package and its version preserved rather than replaced, as charter Section 14.9 requires and Section 5.9 records. Where the ruling adds an obligation to the contract, it reaches the contract only as a versioned revision of the main specification and of this document under their own identities, never as a silent reinterpretation of either. |
| Current status | Unresolved. No ruling exists. |

No further unresolved conformance question exists at this head. The editorial and cross-section integration items the drafting history reserves to Chunk 8 are integration repairs of already-approved text, not conformance questions, and are not recorded here. Section 5.14 of the main specification states that none of its nine carried items is an Open question in the Section 4.3 sense; item 4 is recorded here because the Section 13.9 rationale expressly carries its later question into the completed evidence contract, where it acquires the conformance consequence this register records.

## 8. Conformance acceptance criteria

This section states when a future implementation is accepted as conforming — the second of the three gates Section 20 of the main specification keeps distinct — and it states that gate separately from the other two. It adds no gate of its own: the gates are Section 20's, and this section states how an assessment under this document satisfies the second.

**Separation from documentation-package acceptance.** Work Package 2 documentation-package acceptance is the first Section 20 gate: a complete normative contract, a complete conformance mapping, a complete handoff register, no unresolved blocking contradiction, final Chunk 8 integration, an assigned established specification version, and successful full continuous integration on the exact final head. Completion of this document belongs to that gate's inputs and does not reach the gate: Chunk 7 completion claims no documentation-package acceptance, Chunk 8 remains required, and no established specification version is assigned at this head. Because the exact-version binding of Section 1 names an established specification version, no implementation-acceptance assessment can begin before the first gate completes.

**Implementation acceptance.** An implementation build is accepted as conforming only where all of the following hold, as the second Section 20 gate states.

1. The implementation is separately authorized: approval, completion, or merge of the specification package authorized none, as WP2-SCOPE-014 states and the handoff boundary of Section 18 of the main specification restates for implementation.
2. The assessment is bound to the exact triple of Section 1 — one established main-specification version, one conformance-document version, and one implementation build, each named exactly.
3. Every implementation-acceptance requirement of the Section 2 matrix has a current passing basis from its mapped checks — or, where that requirement's own language permits inapplicability, the explicitly justified inapplicability outcome WP2-HAND-008 permits, exactly as WP2-HAND-016 and WP2-HAND-035 admit — with every requirement of the exact bound specification version accounted for in the coverage report, none omitted, none carried by a blocked, unrun, or evidence-free result, and every inapplicability resting on the recorded basis Section 1 requires.
4. Every suite case of Section 4 shows both its clean baseline pass and successful detection of every required negative or fault case, neither substituting for the other.
5. The complete conformance evidence exists as the sealed, nonsecret, verifiable package Section 5 states, including its complete requirement-coverage report.
6. Every blocking implementation decision of the Section 18 register of the main specification, in the Section 18.2 sense, is resolved and versioned; an unresolved blocking decision leaves the assessment incomplete rather than conditionally passed.
7. The keyless boundary held throughout: no live provider credential, repository game-model call, browser experiment, or Work Package 3 study contributed to any pass, and any later registered-study evidence present is identified as supplementary and substitutes for nothing.

A whole-implementation conformance verdict under these criteria is the infrastructure verdict of Section 1's acceptance-versus-research separation: it makes the implementation eligible for consideration under the third Section 20 gate and establishes nothing that gate reserves.

**Separation from Work Package 3 parity.** Work Package 3 parity eligibility and evidence form the third Section 20 gate: a separately authorized, separately registered, preregistered study conducted after implementation acceptance, never inferred from either earlier gate. Conformance acceptance under this section asserts no parity claim, as Section 6 states and as WP2-SCOPE-011 and WP2-AGG-041 bind for reporting.
